<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\\r\\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\\r\\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\\r\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\r\\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\\r\\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\\r\\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\\r\\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\\r\\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\\r\\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\\r\\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **\\"Joshua\\"**, students display their real profile names.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\\r\\n\\r\\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\\r\\n\\r\\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\\r\\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\\r\\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"493547","wall_time_seconds":0.5006852,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":7505,"output":"import { Link } from \'react-router-dom\';\\r\\nimport { DynamicIslandTOC } from \'@/components/ui/dynamic-island-toc\';\\r\\nimport type { ConditionalUnit } from \'../content/types\';\\r\\nimport { LessonInteraction } from \'./LessonInteractions\';\\r\\nimport \'../notes.css\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface ConditionalLessonNoteProps {\\r\\n  unit: ConditionalUnit;\\r\\n  onComplete: () => void;\\r\\n  continueHref: string;\\r\\n  previousHref?: string;\\r\\n  reviewed: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction sectionTitle(unit: ConditionalUnit, index: number): string {\\r\\n  if (unit.id === \'orientation\') return [\'What a conditional says\', \'A rule needs a case fact\', \'Keep the strength of the claim\'][index] || \'Read the complete guarantee\';\\r\\n  if (unit.id === \'rules-facts\') return [\'Separate the fact and rule\', \'What each fact can establish\', \'A chain produces a new rule\', \'Show the match and explain why\', \'Four facts, one unchanged rule\', \'A requirement is not a completed action\'][index] || \'Check the whole claim\';\\r\\n  if (unit.area === \'translation\') return [\'Read the indicator in context\', \'Preserve the complete ideas\', \'Check the meaning before shortening\', \'Use a meaning check\', \'Practice the translation\', \'Recognize related expressions\', \'Keep the direction precise\'][index] || \'Compare the wording\';\\r\\n  if (unit.area === \'linking\') return [\'Find the exact connection\', \'Keep rules separate from facts\', \'Preserve subjects and scope\', \'Expose a hidden connection\', \'Make the path visible\', \'Check intermediate conclusions\', \'Match the complete requirement\'][index] || \'Test the proposed connection\';\\r\\n  return index === 0 ? \'Understand the relationship\' : index === 1 ? \'Compare the possible cases\' : index === 2 ? \'Keep the whole claim\' : `Check the reasoning ${index - 1}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ConditionalLessonNote({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"conditional-note\\">\\r\\n    <DynamicIslandTOC key={unit.id} selector=\\".conditional-note [data-toc]\\" />\\r\\n    <header className=\\"cn-hero\\"><div><p className=\\"cn-label\\">Conditional logic</p><h1>{unit.title}</h1><p>{unit.goal}</p></div><a className=\\"cn-start\\" href=\\"#conditional-note-start\\">Read the lesson<span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">↓</span></a></header>\\r\\n    <article id=\\"conditional-note-start\\" className=\\"cn-paper\\">\\r\\n      {unit.id === \'orientation\' && <section data-toc data-toc-title=\\"A promise about qualifying cases\\" className=\\"cn-section\\"><p className=\\"cn-label\\">Start with the meaning</p><h2>A promise about qualifying cases</h2><p>A conditional connects two complete ideas. It says: if the starting condition is met, the required result must follow. It does not say that the starting condition has already happened.</p><div className=\\"cn-diagram\\"><span>If this condition holds</span><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span><span>This result must follow</span></div><p>The left side is the trigger. The right side is what the rule guarantees. Understand that promise before memorizing indicator words.</p></section>}\\r\\n      {unit.teaching.map((paragraph, index) => <section key={index} className=\\"cn-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title={sectionTitle(unit, index)}><p className=\\"cn-label\\">{String(index + 1).padStart(2, \'0\')}</p><h2>{sectionTitle(unit, index)}</h2><p>{paragraph}</p></section>)}\\r\\n      <section className=\\"cn-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title=\\"Work through an example\\"><h2>Work through an example</h2><div className=\\"cn-example\\">{unit.workedExample.statements.map((statement, index) => <p key={index}>{statement}</p>)}</div><ol className=\\"cn-reasoning\\">{unit.workedExample.reasoning.map((reason, index) => <li key={index}>{reason}</li>)}</ol><div className=\\"cn-result\\"><p className=\\"cn-label\\">What follows</p><p><strong>{unit.workedExample.conclusion}</strong></p></div></section>\\r\\n      <section className=\\"cn-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title=\\"Try the relationship\\"><h2>Try the relationship</h2><LessonInteraction unit={unit} /></section>\\r\\n      <section className=\\"cn-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title=\\"A method to check your work\\"><h2>A method to check your work</h2><ol className=\\"cn-method\\">{unit.method.map((step, index) => <li key={index}>{step}</li>)}</ol></section>\\r\\n      <section id=\\"repair\\" className=\\"cn-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title=\\"Catch the tempting mistake\\"><h2>Catch the tempting mistake</h2><div className=\\"cn-contrast\\"><div><p className=\\"cn-label\\">The tempting move</p><p>{unit.commonMistake.mistake}</p></div><div><p className=\\"cn-label\\">Check it this way</p><p>{unit.commonMistake.repair}</p></div></div></section>\\r\\n      <footer className=\\"cn-footer\\"><p>{reviewed ? \'You marked this lesson as read. You can revisit it whenever you need.\' : \'Marking this lesson as read records review, not mastery.\'}</p><div className=\\"cn-controls\\">{previousHref && <Link to={previousHref}>Previous lesson</Link>}<button type=\\"button\\" onClick={onComplete}>{reviewed ? \'Lesson read\' : \'Mark lesson as read\'}</button><Link className=\\"cn-primary\\" to={continueHref}>Continue to practice →</Link></div></footer>\\r\\n    </article>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { useState } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport type { ConditionalUnit } from \'../content/types\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst cases = [\\r\\n  { fact: \'The parcel was scanned.\', result: \'Its tracking record was updated.\', why: \'The fact meets the trigger. Apply the guarantee to this parcel.\' },\\r\\n  { fact: \'Its tracking record was not updated.\', result: \'The parcel was not scanned.\', why: \'Scanning would require an update. The missing result rules out scanning.\' },\\r\\n  { fact: \'Its tracking record was updated.\', result: \'Whether the parcel was scanned remains unknown.\', why: \'An update might occur another way. The rule supplies no reverse guarantee.\' },\\r\\n  { fact: \'The parcel was not scanned.\', result: \'Whether its tracking record was updated remains unknown.\', why: \'The rule only guarantees an update when scanning occurs.\' },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function FourCaseExplorer() {\\r\\n  const [active, setActive] = useState(0);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cn-interaction\\">\\r\\n    <p className=\\"cn-label\\">One unchanged rule</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"cn-statement\\">If a parcel is scanned, its tracking record is updated.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-diagram\\"><span>Parcel scanned</span><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span><span>Record updated</span></div>\\r\\n    <p>Change only the fact. Predict what follows, then select that case.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-controls\\">{cases.map((item, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={item.fact} aria-pressed={active === index} onClick={() => setActive(index)}>{item.fact}</button>)}</div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-result\\" aria-live=\\"polite\\"><p className=\\"cn-label\\">What follows</p><p><strong>{cases[active].result}</strong></p><p>{cases[active].why}</p></div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst translations: Record<string, { sentence: string; target: string; rule: string; why: string }> = {\\r\\n  \'group-1\': { sentence: \'A receipt appears whenever a payment is recorded.\', target: \'Whenever what? A payment is recorded.\', rule: \'Payment recorded → receipt appears.\', why: \'The trigger appears second in the sentence. Its position does not reverse the guarantee.\' },\\r\\n  \'group-2\': { sentence: \'A current pass is required for entry.\', target: \'What is required? A current pass.\', rule: \'Entry → current pass.\', why: \'The required idea appears before required. Having a pass alone does not guarantee entry.\' },\\r\\n  \'group-3\': { sentence: \'The lamp does not light unless the battery is charged.\', target: \'The two complete ideas are lamp does not light and battery is charged.\', rule: \'Battery not charged → lamp does not light. Equivalently: lamp lights → battery charged.\', why: \'Keep the existing negative. Negating does not light gives lights when you form the equivalent direction.\' },\\r\\n  \'group-4\': { sentence: \'No approved sample is unlabelled.\', target: \'The excluded pair is approved sample and unlabelled sample.\', rule: \'Approved sample → not unlabelled, or labelled.\', why: \'Negating unlabelled cancels its existing negative. This does not make every labelled sample approved.\' },\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction TranslationExplorer({ id }: { id: string }) {\\r\\n  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const item = translations[id];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cn-interaction\\"><p className=\\"cn-statement\\">{item.sentence}</p><p>Identify the complete ideas and predict the direction before revealing it.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-controls\\"><button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => setStep(Math.max(step, 1))}>Find the target</button><button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => setStep(2)}>Show the translation</button><button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => setStep(0)}>Try again</button></div>\\r\\n    <div aria-live=\\"polite\\">{step >= 1 && <p>{item.target}</p>}{step === 2 && <div className=\\"cn-result\\"><p><strong>{item.rule}</strong></p><p>{item.why}</p></div>}</div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ChainExplorer() {\\r\\n  const [fact, setFact] = useState(false);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cn-interaction\\"><p className=\\"cn-label\\">Two rules</p><div className=\\"cn-diagram\\"><span>Calibrated</span><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span><span>Registered</span><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</span><span>Listed</span></div>\\r\\n    <p>Registered is the exact result-to-trigger match. The rules establish calibrated → listed.</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-controls\\"><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={fact} onClick={() => setFact(!fact)}>{fact ? \'Remove the case fact\' : \'Add: Meter D is calibrated\'}</button></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-result\\" aria-live=\\"polite\\"><p><strong>{fact ? \'Meter D is registered and listed.\' : \'Every calibrated meter is listed.\'}</strong></p><p>{fact ? \'The separate fact activates the chain for D. Both intermediate and final results follow.\' : \'This is a new rule. These rules alone do not establish that any calibrated meter exists.\'}</p></div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction NoLinkExplorer() {\\r\\n  const [sharedResult, setSharedResult] = useState(true);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cn-interaction\\"><p className=\\"cn-label\\">Compare the arrow positions</p>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-diagram\\"><span>Calibrated → registered</span><span>{sharedResult ? \'Inspected → registered\' : \'Registered → listed\'}</span></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-controls\\"><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={sharedResult} onClick={() => setSharedResult(true)}>Both rules end at registered</button><button type=\\"button\\" aria-pressed={!sharedResult} onClick={() => setSharedResult(false)}>The second begins at registered</button></div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-result\\" aria-live=\\"polite\\"><p><strong>{sharedResult ? \'No calibrated → inspected link follows.\' : \'Calibrated → listed follows.\'}</strong></p><p>{sharedResult ? \'Registered is a shared result. A calibrated, uninspected meter can still be registered and fit both rules.\' : \'Registered is now a result-to-trigger match. Follow the two forward arrows without reversing either rule.\'}</p></div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction WorkedReasoningReveal({ unit }: { unit: ConditionalUnit }) {\\r\\n  const [shown, setShown] = useState(0);\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"cn-interaction\\"><p className=\\"cn-label\\">Explain the next move</p><p>Use the statements above. Say what the next step establishes and why before revealing it.</p>\\r\\n    <ol>{unit.workedExample.reasoning.slice(0, shown).map((reason, index) => <li key={index}>{reason}</li>)}</ol>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"cn-controls\\"><button type=\\"button\\" disabled={shown === unit.workedExample.reasoning.length} onClick={() => setShown(shown + 1)}>Reveal next reasoning step</button><button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => setShown(0)}>Start again</button></div>\\r\\n    <div aria-live=\\"polite\\">{shown === unit.workedExample.reasoning.length && <p className=\\"cn-result\\"><strong>{unit.workedExample.conclusion}</strong></p>}</div>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"cn-caption\\">Compare your explanation with the steps. This exploration does not grade your written reasoning.</p>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function LessonInteraction({ unit }: { unit: ConditionalUnit }) {\\r\\n  if (translations[unit.id]) return <TranslationExplorer key={unit.id} id={unit.id} />;\\r\\n  if ([\'orientation\', \'rules-facts\', \'sufficient-necessary\', \'contraposition\', \'triggering\'].includes(unit.id)) return <FourCaseExplorer key={unit.id} />;\\r\\n  if (unit.id === \'no-link\') return <NoLinkExplorer />;\\r\\n  if (unit.area === \'linking\') return <ChainExplorer key={unit.id} />;\\r\\n  return <WorkedReasoningReveal key={unit.id} unit={unit} />;\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { createContext, useContext, useEffect, useState } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { Link, NavLink, Navigate, Outlet, Route, Routes, useLocation, useParams, useSearchParams } from \'react-router-dom\';\\r\\nimport { ArrowLeft, ArrowRight, BookOpen, Clock, RotateCcw } from \'lucide-react\';\\r\\nimport { conditionalUnits, findConditionalUnit, getConditionalQuestions } from \'./content\';\\r\\nimport { DIFFICULTIES, type ConditionalArea, type ConditionalDifficulty, type ConditionalQuestion, type ConditionalUnit } from \'./content/types\';\\r\\nimport { attemptSummary, elapsedSeconds, emptyDraft, newRun, type AnswerDraft, type ConditionalProgress, type ConditionalRun } from \'./progress\';\\r\\nimport { useConditionalProgress } from \'./useConditionalProgress\';\\r\\nimport { PageHeader } from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/components/primitives/PageHeader\';\\r\\nimport { Dialog, DialogContent, \'Saved to your account\' : status === \'loading\' ? \'Loading your saved work...\' : status === \'saving\' ? \'Saving your changes...\' : status === \'unsaved\' ? \'Changes waiting to save\' : \'Changes are not saved\'}</div></header>{error && <div className=\\"cb-error\\" role=\\"alert\\"><p>{error}</p>{progress ? [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 10691)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{"chunk_id":"74e6e0","wall_time_seconds":0.418036,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":10429,"output":"# Conditional Logic: What the Rule Gives You\\r\\n\\r\\nDraft for Joshua\'s review · 2026-10-02\\r\\n\\r\\nThese six lessons form the opening chapter. Each teaches one idea, works through an example, and gives the student a small task before the attached practice. Visual and drill directions for our review are at the end, separate from the student copy. All new practice examples below are original draft examples.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Start with a guarantee\\r\\n\\r\\nTake this statement:\\r\\n\\r\\n> All dogs are mammals.\\r\\n\\r\\nNow suppose I tell you that Luna is a dog. You know something else about Luna: she\'s a mammal.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou didn\'t need another fact about her. The rule already covers every dog, and Luna falls inside that group.\\r\\n\\r\\nWe can say the same rule another way:\\r\\n\\r\\n> If something is a dog, then it is a mammal.\\r\\n\\r\\nThat\'s a conditional. It tells you that **when one condition is met, another must be met too.**\\r\\n\\r\\nNotice the guarantee. The statement doesn\'t say dogs are usually mammals. It leaves no room for a dog that isn\'t a mammal.\\r\\n\\r\\nWe can write the relationship like this:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Dog → Mammal**\\r\\n\\r\\nRead the arrow as “if this is true, then that must be true.”\\r\\n\\r\\nThe rule doesn\'t tell us that a particular dog exists. It tells us what must be true of anything that is a dog. When we add the fact about Luna, we can apply the rule to her.\\r\\n\\r\\n### See the relationship\\r\\n\\r\\nImagine a box containing every mammal. Every dog has to fit inside that box. Mammals can sit outside the dog box, but dogs can\'t sit outside the mammal box.\\r\\n\\r\\nThat\'s why knowing “dog” gives us “mammal.”\\r\\n\\r\\n### Your turn\\r\\n\\r\\n> All volunteers wear badges. Ari is a volunteer.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat does the rule guarantee about Ari?\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>See the reasoning</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\nAri wears a badge. The rule covers every volunteer, and Ari is a volunteer. **Volunteer → Wears a badge.**\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep this:** A conditional tells you what must follow when its condition is met.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Why the direction matters\\r\\n\\r\\nLet\'s stay with the same rule:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Dog → Mammal**\\r\\n\\r\\nIf I tell you Luna is a dog, you know she\'s a mammal.\\r\\n\\r\\nBut if I tell you Luna is a mammal, do you know she\'s a dog? No. She could be a cat. The rule has room for mammals that aren\'t dogs.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe arrow gives us a direction. Switching that direction changes the claim.\\r\\n\\r\\nThat\'s a major part of conditional reasoning on the LSAT. You need to know what a rule gives you, and where someone has gone beyond it.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn a Must Be True question, you may apply a rule to a fact. In a Flaw question, someone may reverse the rule and claim more than the premises establish. In a Sufficient Assumption question, you may need a missing rule that connects the evidence to the conclusion.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe question changes. The relationship still matters.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Compare the two moves\\r\\n\\r\\n| What we know | What we can conclude | Why |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Luna is a dog. | Luna is a mammal. | The rule covers every dog. |\\r\\n| Luna is a mammal. | We don\'t know whether Luna is a dog. | Other mammals are allowed. |\\r\\n\\r\\n### Your turn\\r\\n\\r\\n> Every volunteer wears a badge. Ari wears a badge.\\r\\n\\r\\nDoes that establish that Ari is a volunteer? Explain your answer using the rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>See the reasoning</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\nNo. The rule requires volunteers to wear badges. It doesn\'t say that only volunteers wear them. Ari could be a staff member who also wears a badge.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep this:** Follow the direction the rule gives you. Before accepting a conclusion, check that the rule actually reaches it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Sufficient means enough\\r\\n\\r\\nWe have names for the two sides of a conditional. Start with the left:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Dog → Mammal**\\r\\n\\r\\nBeing a dog is **sufficient** for being a mammal. It\'s enough. Once we know something is a dog, we have what we need to conclude that it\'s a mammal.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou can think of the sufficient condition as the trigger. When it is met, the rule gives you the other side.\\r\\n\\r\\n> If a student earns full credit, that student passes the assignment.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Earns full credit → Passes**\\r\\n\\r\\nEarning full credit is sufficient for passing. Suppose Maya earned full credit. Put yourself in her situation: under this rule, what do you know? She passes.\\r\\n\\r\\nBut full credit might not be the only way to pass. A student could earn less than full credit and still pass. “Enough” doesn\'t mean “the only way.”\\r\\n\\r\\n### Your turn\\r\\n\\r\\n> If a person wins the race, that person receives a medal.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich condition is enough to guarantee the other? Then write the arrow.\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>See the reasoning</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\nWinning is enough to guarantee receiving a medal. **Wins → Receives a medal.** The rule doesn\'t tell us that winning is the only way to receive one.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep this:** The sufficient condition goes on the left. Meeting it is enough to guarantee the right.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Necessary means required\\r\\n\\r\\nNow look at the other side:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Dog → Mammal**\\r\\n\\r\\nBeing a mammal is **necessary** for being a dog. A dog has to be a mammal.\\r\\n\\r\\nNecessary means required. It doesn\'t mean enough.\\r\\n\\r\\nReturn to the assignment:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Earns full credit → Passes**\\r\\n\\r\\nUnder this rule, passing is necessary for earning full credit. You can\'t earn full credit and fail. But knowing someone passed doesn\'t tell you they earned full credit.\\r\\n\\r\\nHere\'s a different example:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Entering the archive requires a pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Enters the archive → Has a pass**\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pass is required. That\'s why it goes on the right. Having a pass doesn\'t, by itself, establish that someone enters the archive.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Put the two sides together\\r\\n\\r\\n| Sufficient | Necessary |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Enough to guarantee the other condition. | Required whenever the sufficient condition is met. |\\r\\n| Goes on the left. | Goes on the right. |\\r\\n| If I have this, what must follow? | What must I have in order for the left side to be true? |\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are two roles in the same relationship. You don\'t decide that a condition is always sufficient or always necessary. You read the particular rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Your turn\\r\\n\\r\\n> Every finalist receives an invitation.\\r\\n\\r\\nIdentify the sufficient condition and the necessary condition. Does receiving an invitation establish that someone is a finalist?\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>See the reasoning</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\nBeing a finalist is sufficient. Receiving an invitation is necessary. **Finalist → Receives an invitation.** Receiving one doesn\'t establish finalist status; the rule permits other people to receive invitations too.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep this:** Sufficient is enough. Necessary is required. Required means right.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Group 1: Find the sufficient idea\\r\\n\\r\\nYou already understand the relationship. Now we\'re going to recognize the different ways a sentence can express it.\\r\\n\\r\\nOur Group 1 indicators are:\\r\\n\\r\\n**if · when · where · all · the only · every · any**\\r\\n\\r\\nThese introduce the sufficient idea. That complete idea goes on the left of the arrow.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe important question is: **“What is the indicator referring to?”**\\r\\n\\r\\n### Start with “if”\\r\\n\\r\\n> If a student revises the essay, the student receives feedback.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf what? If the student revises the essay.\\r\\n\\r\\nThat\'s the sufficient idea:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Revises the essay → Receives feedback**\\r\\n\\r\\nNow move the same clause:\\r\\n\\r\\n> A student receives feedback if the student revises the essay.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf what? Still, if the student revises the essay. The arrow stays the same. Sentence order changed; the relationship didn\'t.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The same relationship in other words\\r\\n\\r\\n| Sentence | What the indicator refers to | Translation |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Every volunteer wears a badge. | Every volunteer. | Volunteer → Wears a badge |\\r\\n| Any applicant who submits late pays a fee. | Any applicant who submits late. | Applicant submits late → Pays a fee |\\r\\n| When the alarm sounds, the staff evacuate. | When the alarm sounds. | Alarm sounds → Staff evacuate |\\r\\n| Where there is smoke, there is fire. | Where there is smoke. | Smoke → Fire |\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the complete idea. In the applicant sentence, “applicant” alone isn\'t enough. The rule applies to an applicant **who submits late**.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Read “the only” carefully\\r\\n\\r\\n> The only people admitted are members.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe only what? The only people admitted.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Admitted → Member**\\r\\n\\r\\nEveryone admitted must be a member. It doesn\'t tell us that every member is admitted.\\r\\n\\r\\nDon\'t skip “the.” We\'ll compare “the only” with “only” in the next lesson.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Your turn\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each sentence, identify the indicator, ask what it refers to, and write the complete rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. All registered guests receive a wristband.\\r\\n2. A report is reviewed if it is submitted before noon.\\r\\n3. The only vehicles permitted inside are electric vehicles.\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>See the reasoning</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **All** refers to registered guests. Registered guest → Receives a wristband.\\r\\n2. **If** refers to submission before noon. Submitted before noon → Reviewed.\\r\\n3. **The only** refers to vehicles permitted inside. Vehicle permitted inside → Electric vehicle.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep this:** Find the indicator\'s target. For Group 1, that complete idea goes left.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Group 2: Find what\'s required\\r\\n\\r\\nGroup 2 introduces the necessary idea. That complete idea goes on the right.\\r\\n\\r\\nOur Group 2 indicators are:\\r\\n\\r\\n**only · only if · only when · only where · always · requires · must**\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the same question: **“What is the indicator referring to?”**\\r\\n\\r\\n### Start with “only if”\\r\\n\\r\\n> A student enters the archive only if the student has a pass.\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly if what? Only if the student has a pass.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pass is required:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Enters the archive → Has a pass**\\r\\n\\r\\nCompare that with “if”:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Sentence | Rule |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A student enters if the student has a pass. | Has a pass → Enters |\\r\\n| A student enters only if the student has a pass. | Enters → Has a pass |\\r\\n\\r\\nThe first makes a pass enough. The second makes a pass required. One word changes what the sentence guarantees.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Required things can appear first\\r\\n\\r\\n> A pass is required for entry.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat is required? A pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Enters → Has a pass**\\r\\n\\r\\nThe necessary idea appears before “required.” That\'s why we ask what the indicator refers to instead of automatically taking the words after it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Compare “only” and “the only”\\r\\n\\r\\n> Only members are admitted.\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly what? Only members. Being a member is required for admission.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Admitted → Member**\\r\\n\\r\\n> The only people admitted are members.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe only what? The only people admitted. That is the sufficient idea.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Admitted → Member**\\r\\n\\r\\nThese two sentences express the same rule. The indicator points to a different part of each sentence. Read the whole statement before deciding where each idea belongs.\\r\\n\\r\\n### More ways to state a requirement\\r\\n\\r\\n| Sentence | Required idea | Translation |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| A finalist must attend the interview. | Attends the interview. | Finalist → Attends interview |\\r\\n| Approval requires a signature. | Has a signature. | Approved → Has signature |\\r\\n| A review occurs only when a complaint is filed. | A complaint is filed. | Review occurs → Complaint filed |\\r\\n| The service operates only where a station is available. | A station is available. | Service operates → Station available |\\r\\n| A finalist always receives an invitation. | Receives an invitation. | Finalist → Receives invitation |\\r\\n\\r\\nHere, “always” expresses a rule without exceptions. Read it in context; a word by itself doesn\'t give you the complete relationship.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Your turn\\r\\n\\r\\nIdentify what\'s required, then write each rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Only registered guests receive wristbands.\\r\\n2. A permit is required for construction.\\r\\n3. A student graduates only if the student completes the project.\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>See the reasoning</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Registration is required. Receives wristband → Registered guest.\\r\\n2. A permit is required. Construction → Has permit.\\r\\n3. Completing the project is required. Graduates → Completes project.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep this:** For Group 2, the required idea goes right. Ask what the indicator refers to, then check the meaning of your arrow against the sentence.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## For our review: How the teaching and practice fit together\\r\\n\\r\\nThis section is editorial planning, not student-facing copy. Each lesson ends with its own drill before the student continues. Joshua can assign a whole chapter or an individual lesson and its practice. Students see their assigned work and its next step.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Lesson | Visual or guided interaction | Attached drill | What it establishes |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 1. Guarantee | Reuse the nested-box activity shown in the supplied screenshot if its existing implementation correctly validates containment. Place dogs inside mammals; then place Luna inside dogs. No strict size claim: sets may be equal. | Distinguish guaranteed rules from “usually” claims; apply one explicit rule to a matching fact. | Student understands a guarantee and rule versus fact. |\\r\\n| 2. Direction | Place a cat inside mammals but outside dogs. Compare what the two starting facts allow. | Rule + fact: choose follows or undetermined, then explain. | Student applies the rule without reversing it. |\\r\\n| 3. Sufficient | Reveal “passes” when “earns full credit” is selected. Show a separate permitted example of passing without full credit. | Identify what is enough in plain if/then rules; apply it. | Student understands enough without assuming only way. |\\r\\n| 4. Necessary | Highlight the required pass in an entry example; compare a person with a pass who stays outside. | Identify both roles; distinguish required from enough. | Student can explain both sides of one rule. |\\r\\n| 5. Group 1 | Highlight indicator → select complete target → place target left → supply right. Move the if-clause without changing the rule. | Short blocks by indicator, then mixed Group 1 translations; include complete modifiers and “the only.” | Student translates every Group 1 form, beyond keyword recognition. |\\r\\n| 6. Group 2 | Same interaction with target placed right; place “a pass” before “required”; compare if/only if and only/the only. | Short blocks by indicator, then mixed Group 1/2 translations in both sentence orders. | Student interprets requirements without positional shortcuts. |\\r\\n\\r\\nDrill scaffolding fades: first show a worked example, then give a target-selection prompt, then require an independent translation. Feedback explains the student\'s specific directional or target error. Keep initial learning untimed. Timing can be added to teacher-assigned medium and advanced practice later, as previously agreed.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are six opening lessons, not six difficulty levels. Contraposition, Groups 3 and 4, compound conditions, chaining, and LSAT application belong in subsequent lessons. Quantities remain the next phase after the conditional curriculum.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Reference and verification limits\\r\\n\\r\\n- Joshua\'s voice: `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.\\r\\n- Group lists and What Test: `corpus/Curriculum/Conditional Logic/Notes/_megadoc_notes_conditionals.md`; current app `src/features/conditional-refresher/content.ts`. The corpus contains older variants, so this draft uses the current seven-item lists for each group.\\r\\n- Existing practice reference: `corpus/Curriculum/Conditional Logic/Notes/homework_conditionals.md`. Audit and reuse suitable existing items before adding replacements.\\r\\n- Intro to LR: current `IntroLessonPage.tsx`, `IntroNotesShell.tsx`, and `NoteInteractions.tsx`, inspected in source. The live reference tab did not return readable page content, so visual parity has not been verified in the browser.\\r\\n- NotebookLM: the three source transcripts and generated reports reviewed in `2026-10-02-opening-lessons-source-map.md`. Use their conceptual explanations as reference; generated mistakes and unsupported numerical claims were excluded.\\r\\n- This file is a prose and practice draft. It has not been published, implemented, or assigned to students.\\r\\n70:Figuring Out The Language. Lets tackle the biggest challenge in conditional reasoning figuring out the language. Well start by returning to our first example and pointing out the target of each indicator word. The indicators target is the word or phrase it is referring to. If Im breathing then air is present. Each instance of breathing has air. When Im breathing air is present. Im breathing only if air is present. Any breathing means air is present. Only the presence of air allows for breathing. All instances of breathing have air present. Breathing depends on air being present. Every instance of breathing has air present. I need air to breathe. In order to breathe I must have air. I have to have air if Im breathing. People who breathe need air. Notice that in each of these examples the indicator is right before its target. This will be the case in the majority of conditional statements you read but acting on the generalization that the indicator is always immediately before its target will lead you into trouble. Air must be present for breathing. Air is essential for breathing. Air is necessary for breathing. Air is a precondition for breathing. Air is required for breathing. In these examples the target precedes the indicator. Why do they have to make it so difficult you ask? Because the LSAT wants you to understand what the sentence is really saying. They dont want you to see an indicator word and go on autopilot. You also cannot generalize that these specific necessary indicators will always come after their target. Each of them can switch sides. THE WHAT TEST. The way you can get around all this ambiguity is by taking the time to actually understand what the sentence is telling you. When you see an indicator and you have even a little doubt about its target ask yourself one question: What is the indicator referring to? The What Test is simple. You can perform it by adding a what to whichever side of the indicator makes grammatical sense. Heres how you perform the What Test: If what? When what? Any what? All what? Every what? In order to what? People who what? Each what? Must what? Whats necessary? Whats required? Only if what? Depends on what? Need what? Have to what? Whats essential? Whats the precondition? The What Test will probably not be necessary for the indicators like if and all because their target is clear. With some of the more confusing necessary indicators like depends and required you may use the What Test more. Air is required for breathing. I require air to breathe. The What Test probably seems simple to you and it is. However it is only simple because Im taking the split second to actually understand the sentence and figure out what require is referring to. You can do this too just be sure to take your time with conditionals in the beginning and youll be lightning quick in a couple weeks. Your Friend the Personalized If/Then. Personalized if/thens are translations for conditionals. Heres another safeguard on conditional diagramming. Make a habit of translating conditionals into personalized if/thens. Personalized if/thens require you to put yourself in the con…691 tokens truncated…icient condition. 3. Put yourself in the situation of your guess and ask what you know about yourself. 4. If your guess is correct you will find that the necessary condition has to pertain. Example Translation into Personalized If/Then. Original Statement: Orchids depend on frequent conversation. Casual Meaning: Orchids need frequent conversation. Guess: Orchids as the sufficient condition. Personalization: If Im an orchid what do I know about me? I depend on frequent conversation. If Im an orchid then I have frequent conversation. This method not only aids in understanding the conditional but also verifies its correctness. Incorrect guesses can be easily identified and corrected through this personal context. Advantages of Personalized If/Then. Helps intuitively understand conditionals by imagining yourself as part of the scenario. Provides confidence throughout tests especially in correctly interpreting conditionals not phrased as if/then statements. Both the What Test and Personalized If/Then Strategy are crucial tools in deciphering and applying conditional logic particularly in competitive exams like the LSAT.\\r\\n214:Page 1 Conditional Logic Workbook Parts I–V · LSAT Conditional Reasoning (Unified Edition) Name: ________________________________ Date: ________________________________ Page 2 Conditional Indicators Matrix Group 1 — Universals: all, every, any, each Group 2 — “Only” Family: only, only if, only when, only where Group 3 — Unless Set: unless, until, without Group 4 — Negatives/Restrictions: no, none, never, cannot, not both, except Page 3 PART I Mixed Conditionals (Translation) Page 4 How to Transl\\r\\nimport type { ConditionalQuestion, ConditionalUnit } from \'./types\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Original LSAT U drafts, 2026-10-01. Pedagogical provenance: ai-os/wiki/teaching-voice.md,\\r\\n * ai-os/wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md, and ai-os/brainstorms/2026-09-30-conditional-curriculum/.\\r\\n * Group taxonomy is taken from src/features/conditional-refresher/content.ts.\\r\\n * Meaning checks and the six-step scaffold follow Joshua\'s archived Conditional\\r\\n * Understanding, intuition technique when stuck, and 6 step process notes.\\r\\n * Fact/rule contrasts follow Yahunatan\'s current V10 recap and Sofia\'s\\r\\n * 2026-09-11 Conditional MBT draft. Their local draft status is not proof of posting.\\r\\n * Recall and Rephrase instructions are an authored adaptation of archived homework\\r\\n * naming that exercise; the archive does not supply its complete original procedure.\\r\\n * Extended expression families also reference Asia\'s assigned 2026-08-24\\r\\n * Conditional Logic Cheat Sheet and The Breakdown; contextual meaning takes\\r\\n * precedence over their mechanical word-list shortcuts.\\r\\n * All scenarios, questions and explanations below are newly authored, not official LSAT items.\\r\\n * Difficulty concerns interpretation and inference. These sets prescribe no mastery threshold or clock.\\r\\n */\\r\\nconst question = (\\r\\n  id: string, prompt: string, options: string[], answerIndex: number,\\r\\n  hint: string, reasoningSteps: string[],\\r\\n): ConditionalQuestion => ({ id, prompt, options, answerIndex, hint, reasoningSteps, explanation: reasoningSteps.join(\' \') });\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const foundationUnits: ConditionalUnit[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'orientation\', title: \'Find the Guarantee\', area: \'understanding\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [],\\r\\n    goal: \'Separate a guarantee, a case fact and a prediction before drawing a conclusion.\',\\r\\n    teaching: [\\r\\n      \'A conditional rule tells you what follows when a condition is met. If a parcel is scanned at intake, its tracking record is updated. Scanning is the starting condition. Updating the record is the guaranteed result.\',\\r\\n      \'The rule does not tell you that any parcel was scanned. A case fact supplies that information: Parcel 18 was scanned at intake. Together, the rule and the fact establish that Parcel 18\\\\\'s tracking record was updated.\',\\r\\n      \'Probably and usually express weaker claims. They do not give an exceptionless guarantee. Keep the whole claim, including its limits. An unknown result is not a false result.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    method: [\'Underline what the statement promises.\', \'Separate any rule from a fact about one case.\', \'Match the fact to the complete starting condition.\', \'State only the promised result about that case.\'],\\r\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every calibrated gauge receives a blue seal.\', \'Gauge 6 is calibrated.\'], reasoning: [\'The rule applies to calibrated gauges.\', \'Gauge 6 meets that condition.\'], conclusion: \'Gauge 6 receives a blue seal.\' },\\r\\n    guidedExample: question(\'orientation-guide\', \'If a card is activated, its number is registered. Card L is activated. What follows?\', [\'Card L\\\\\'s number is registered.\', \'Every registered number belongs to an activated card.\', \'No other card is registered.\'], 0, \'Match Card L to the condition after if.\', [\'Card L satisfies the activation condition.\', \'The rule guarantees registration for that card.\']),\\r\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Treating a rule as evidence that its starting condition occurred.\', repair: \'Read the rule as a promise about qualifying cases. Look separately for a fact identifying one.\' },\\r\\n    drills: {\\r\\n      beginner: [\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-b1\', \'Which statement gives an exceptionless rule?\', [\'The desk is staffed now.\', \'If the desk is staffed, the bell is answered.\', \'The bell is usually answered.\'], 1, \'Look for a guaranteed connection.\', [\'The if statement links staffing to answering.\', \'The other statements report a case or a tendency.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-b2\', \'Every numbered crate has a label. Which additional fact lets you conclude that Crate M has a label?\', [\'Crate M is numbered.\', \'Crate M is heavy.\', \'Crate M might be numbered.\'], 0, \'Find a fact that meets the entire starting condition.\', [\'Being numbered is enough for a label.\', \'The first fact establishes that condition for Crate M.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-b3\', \'Which claim reports a case fact?\', [\'All restored books have new bindings.\', \'Book 4 has a new binding.\', \'Restored books generally look newer.\'], 1, \'A case fact identifies a particular subject.\', [\'Book 4 is one specified book.\', \'The statement reports its binding rather than a general relationship.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      medium: [\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-m1\', \'Most inspected lifts operate quietly. Lift 2 was inspected. Is quiet operation guaranteed?\', [\'Yes.\', \'No; quiet operation is impossible.\', \'No; it remains unsettled.\'], 2, \'Most allows exceptions.\', [\'The claim permits some inspected lifts to operate noisily.\', \'Nothing identifies which kind Lift 2 is.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-m2\', \'If a roof has a solar panel, it has a support frame. A roof has a support frame. What is established?\', [\'It has a solar panel.\', \'It has no solar panel.\', \'Its panel status is unknown.\'], 2, \'The promised result does not establish its source.\', [\'The rule promises a frame for panel-equipped roofs.\', \'It does not exclude frames on other roofs.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-m3\', \'Every sealed sample is logged. No sample is said to be sealed. Which conclusion follows?\', [\'A logged sample exists.\', \'Every sealed sample is logged, but existence remains unknown.\', \'No samples are logged.\'], 1, \'Distinguish a relationship from an existing member.\', [\'The rule governs any sealed sample.\', \'It does not assert that a sample of that kind exists.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      advanced: [\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-a1\', \'Every visitor authorized to enter the archive may request a copy. Imani is a visitor authorized to enter the archive. What is guaranteed?\', [\'Imani requests a copy.\', \'Imani may request a copy.\', \'Imani receives a copy.\'], 1, \'Permission is not an action or a completed outcome.\', [\'The rule grants permission to authorized visitors.\', \'It says neither that Imani uses it nor that a request succeeds.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-a2\', \'Every sensor certified for underwater use has a sealed housing. Sensor K is certified for indoor use. What follows about its housing?\', [\'It is sealed.\', \'It is not sealed.\', \'Neither conclusion is established.\'], 2, \'Compare the two certification scopes.\', [\'Indoor certification does not establish underwater certification.\', \'The supplied rule therefore gives no housing result for K.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'orientation-a3\', \'The manual says: If the warning light flashes, shut down the machine. The light flashes. What follows from this instruction alone?\', [\'The machine has shut down.\', \'The instruction calls for shutdown.\', \'The machine cannot shut down.\'], 1, \'Keep an instruction separate from compliance.\', [\'The flashing light satisfies the instruction\\\\\'s condition.\', \'That establishes the required response, not whether anyone performed it.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'rules-facts\', title: \'Facts, Rules, and What Follows\', area: \'understanding\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'orientation\'],\\r\\n    goal: \'Apply rules to case facts and distinguish case conclusions from new rules.\',\\r\\n    teaching: [\\r\\n      \'A fact tells you something about a case. A rule connects conditions. Every digitized map is searchable is a rule. Map R is digitized is a fact. Match the fact to the rule\\\\\'s starting condition to conclude that Map R is searchable.\',\\r\\n      \'For digitized → searchable, digitized establishes searchable. Not searchable rules out digitized: a digitized map would have to be searchable. Searchable alone leaves digitization unknown. Not digitized alone leaves searchability unknown.\',\\r\\n      \'Two rules can create a new rule. Digitized → searchable and searchable → indexed give digitized → indexed. They do not establish that an indexed map exists. Add a digitized case as a separate fact to establish an indexed case.\',\\r\\n      \'Make the reasoning visible: Fact: Map R is digitized. Rule: every digitized map is searchable. Match: the fact meets the complete digitized-map condition for R. Conclusion: R is searchable. Why: the rule covers that same map. Contrast this with two rules alone: digitized → searchable → indexed proves a new rule, without proving a fact about R.\',\\r\\n      \'Keep one rule unchanged and vary only the fact: translated notice → archived notice. Notice H is translated: H is archived. H is not archived: H is not translated. H is archived: translation remains unknown. H is not translated: archiving remains unknown. In the last two cases, give an allowed example rather than forcing a conclusion.\',\\r\\n      \'Requires approval does not mean has received approval. A late submission may require approval while it is still waiting for review. If the next rule begins with received approval, the requirement does not activate it. Preserve the whole condition before comparing the fact and rule.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    method: [\'List rules separately from case facts.\', \'Keep the same subject and exact predicates when matching.\', \'Use a present trigger or an absent required result.\', \'Label the output as a case conclusion or a conditional rule.\'],\\r\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every waterproof notebook has coated pages.\', \'Notebook A does not have coated pages.\'], reasoning: [\'Waterproof status would require coated pages.\', \'That required result is absent for A.\'], conclusion: \'Notebook A is not waterproof.\' },\\r\\n    guidedExample: question(\'rules-facts-guide\', \'Every translated notice is archived. Notice H is archived. Before choosing, identify the fact, rule, and match. State your conclusion and explain why it follows or remains open. What follows about translation?\', [\'H is translated.\', \'H is not translated.\', \'Translation is unknown.\'], 2, \'Archiving is the required result, not the trigger. An untranslated archived notice would fit the rule.\', [\'Translated notices must be archived.\', \'Other notices may also be archived, so the fact settles neither translation nor its absence.\']),\\r\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Calling an allowed case a proven case.\', repair: \'Ask whether every situation fitting the information includes the conclusion. If both outcomes fit, say unknown.\' },\\r\\n    drills: {\\r\\n      beginner: [\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-b1\', \'Every tested battery has a sticker. Battery P was tested. What follows?\', [\'P has a sticker.\', \'P is the only tested battery.\', \'All stickered batteries were tested.\'], 0, \'Apply the rule to P without adding a reverse rule.\', [\'P meets the tested condition.\', \'The sticker follows for P alone.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-b2\', \'Every polished lens is clear. Lens J is not clear. What follows?\', [\'J is polished.\', \'J is not polished.\', \'J is clear after all.\'], 1, \'Could a polished lens lack the required clarity?\', [\'Polishing would require clarity.\', \'J lacks clarity, so J cannot be polished under the rule.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-b3\', \'Every reserved seat has a marker. Seat W is not reserved. Its marker status is:\', [\'Guaranteed present.\', \'Guaranteed absent.\', \'Unknown.\'], 2, \'The rule speaks only about reserved seats.\', [\'An unreserved seat does not activate the rule.\', \'A marker may be present or absent without breaking it.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      medium: [\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-m1\', \'Every encrypted file is restricted. Every restricted file is logged. No case fact is supplied. Which follows?\', [\'Some logged files exist.\', \'Every encrypted file is logged.\', \'Every logged file is encrypted.\'], 1, \'Match the shared restricted condition.\', [\'Encryption leads to restriction, which leads to logging.\', \'The resulting rule does not assert a file exists.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-m2\', \'Every approved replacement part is stocked. Part D is a replacement part, but approval is unknown. What follows?\', [\'D is stocked.\', \'D is not stocked.\', \'Stocking remains unknown.\'], 2, \'Replacement part and approved replacement part differ.\', [\'The trigger includes approval.\', \'The supplied fact does not meet that entire trigger.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-m3\', \'Every public hearing is recorded. Hearing N is recorded. A student concludes it is public. Which repair is accurate?\', [\'Recorded → public was never given.\', \'Public hearings cannot be recorded.\', \'The rule has no starting condition.\'], 0, \'Identify the direction the student added.\', [\'The rule runs from public status to recording.\', \'The student used its unsupported reverse.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      advanced: [\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-a1\', \'Every eligible applicant may appeal. Tavi is an eligible applicant. Which follows?\', [\'Tavi has appealed.\', \'Tavi may appeal.\', \'Tavi\\\\\'s appeal was accepted.\'], 1, \'Preserve the modal word may.\', [\'Tavi satisfies the complete eligible-applicant condition, which grants appeal permission.\', \'Permission establishes neither action nor acceptance.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-a2\', \'Every licensed guide must carry a radio. Guide Z carries no radio. What follows about compliance?\', [\'Z cannot be licensed in real life.\', \'If Z is licensed, Z violates the stated requirement.\', \'Z must be unlicensed in real life.\'], 1, \'A requirement can be violated; it is not a physical impossibility.\', [\'A licensed Z would be required to carry a radio.\', \'The fact would show noncompliance, rather than proving absence of a license.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'rules-facts-a3\', \'Every shortlisted design is displayed. Every displayed design is catalogued. Design E is not catalogued. What follows?\', [\'E is shortlisted.\', \'E is not shortlisted.\', \'No design is displayed.\'], 1, \'Trace what shortlisting would force for E.\', [\'Shortlisting would lead through display to cataloguing.\', \'E lacks the final required result, so E is not shortlisted.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'sufficient-necessary\', title: \'Enough to Guarantee, Required to Follow\', area: \'understanding\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'rules-facts\'],\\r\\n    goal: \'Identify sufficient and necessary roles and test the four case combinations.\',\\r\\n    teaching: [\\r\\n      \'If a window is impact-rated, it has reinforced glass. Impact-rated is sufficient for reinforced glass: it is enough to guarantee it. Reinforced glass is necessary for impact-rated status: the rating cannot occur without it under this descriptive rule.\',\\r\\n      \'Sufficient and necessary name roles in a particular relationship. Reinforced glass may be the sufficient condition of a different rule. It is not permanently a necessary condition.\',\\r\\n      \'The single forbidden combination is impact-rated with no reinforced glass. The other three combinations fit the rule. Reinforced glass alone does not prove the rating. No rating does not prove no reinforced glass.\',\\r\\n      \'Use the Personalized If/Then when the direction feels unclear. First understand the ordinary meaning. Guess which complete idea is sufficient. Put yourself in that situation and ask what the sentence forces you to know. Test that guess against the wording: if nothing forces the other idea, your proposed direction is not justified.\',\\r\\n      \'A recovery code is required for a verified account. Imagine that I have a verified account. Under this rule, I must have a recovery code. Now imagine that I merely have a recovery code. The sentence does not force me to be verified. Personalizing checks the relationship; it does not add facts or make a guess true.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    method: [\'Read the ordinary meaning before guessing the sufficient condition.\', \'Use a Personalized If/Then: imagine meeting that condition and ask what the wording forces you to know.\', \'Name the sufficient and necessary conditions completely; test rather than assume your guessed direction.\', \'Look for a trigger paired with an absent required result, and distinguish an allowed case from an established fact.\'],\\r\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Every certified helmet has an inner liner.\', \'A helmet has an inner liner but is not certified.\'], reasoning: [\'Certification requires a liner.\', \'The rule does not require every lined helmet to be certified.\'], conclusion: \'This combination fits the rule; it does not reverse it.\' },\\r\\n    guidedExample: question(\'sufficient-necessary-guide\', \'Every verified account has a recovery code. Before choosing, use a Personalized If/Then: imagine that your account is verified and state what you must know about it. Which combination violates the rule?\', [\'Verified, with a recovery code.\', \'Verified, without a recovery code.\', \'Unverified, with a recovery code.\'], 1, \'If my account is verified, the rule forces it to have a recovery code.\', [\'Verification is sufficient for a recovery code.\', \'A verified account without one breaks that guarantee.\']),\\r\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Treating a necessary condition as sufficient.\', repair: \'A required ingredient need not be a complete recipe. Ask whether the reverse relationship was actually supplied.\' },\\r\\n    drills: {\\r\\n      beginner: [\\r\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-b1\', \'If a cabinet is fire-rated, it has a steel shell. Which condition does this rule identify as guaranteeing a steel shell?\', [\'Being fire-rated.\', \'Having a steel shell.\', \'Not being fire-rated.\'], 0, \'Find the condition the if clause presents as its trigger.\', [\'Fire-rated status is the condition the rule presents as sufficient.\', \'It guarantees the steel-shell result; the result itself is not the starting condition identified by this rule.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'sufficient-necessary-b2\', \'Every accredited course has an assessment. What is necessary for accreditation?\', [\'Absence of an assessment.\', \'Having an assessment.\', \'Being an evening course.\'], 1, \'What must an accredited course have?\', [\'Accreditation cannot occur without an assessment under the rule.\', \'An assessment is therefore necessary, though not stated sufficient.\']),\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"eb0bcb","wall_time_seconds":0.4077851,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2522,"output":"Get-Content: \\r\\nLine |\\r\\n   2 |  Get-Content src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/components/intro-notes/Intr …\\r\\n     |  ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\\r\\n     | Cannot find path \'C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os-projects\\\\lsatu-conditional-bootcamp\\\\src\\\\bootcamps\\\\main-conclusion\\\\components\\\\intro-notes\\\\IntroLessonPage.tsx\' because it does not exist.\\r\\n---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-18\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\" — \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Best,\\r\\n.conditional-note{max-width:1040px;margin:auto;color:#24231f;background:#f7f4ee;padding:24px;line-height:1.75;overflow-wrap:anywhere}\\r\\n.conditional-note [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"de0cd5","wall_time_seconds":0.2761757,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":592,"output":"\\r\\n## Source: 6 step processs.pdf\\r\\n\\r\\n**Path:** LSAT DUMP/LR/Conditional/Notes/6 step processs.pdf  **Modified:** 2024-10-21T17:24:57+00:00  **Words:** 42  **Doc ID:** None\\r\\n\\r\\nSteps to Analyze Conditional Statements: 1. Identify the logical operator. 2. Identify the two main concepts (groups, categories, events, ideas). 3. Assign symbols to the two main concepts. 4. Apply the translation rule. 5. Find the contrapositive. 6. Translate back to English.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Source: Conditional Logic LSAT question intro.pdf\\r\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\main-conclusion\\\\modules\\\\lessons\\\\notes\\\\NoteInteractions.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\main-conclusion\\\\modules\\\\lessons\\\\notes\\\\IntroNotesShell.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\main-conclusion\\\\modules\\\\lessons\\\\notes\\\\IntroLessonPage.tsx\\n// Content for the Conditional Indicators Refresher.\\r\\n// Kept as plain data so the page components stay presentational.\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type GroupId = \\"g1\\" | \\"g2\\" | \\"g3\\" | \\"g4\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface GroupDef {\\r\\n  id: GroupId;\\r\\n  n: number;\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  words: string[];\\r\\n  rule?: string;\\r\\n  steps?: string[];\\r\\n  note?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const GROUPS: GroupDef[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \\"g1\\",\\r\\n    n: 1,\\r\\n    title: \\"Sufficient\\",\\r\\n    words: [\\"if\\", \\"when\\", \\"where\\", \\"all\\", \\"the only\\", \\"every\\", \\"any\\"],\\r\\n    rule: \\"The indicator introduces the sufficient idea. That complete idea goes left of the arrow.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \\"g2\\",\\r\\n    n: 2,\\r\\n    title: \\"Necessary\\",\\r\\n    words: [\\"only\\", \\"only if\\", \\"only when\\", \\"only where\\", \\"always\\", \\"requires\\", \\"must\\"],\\r\\n    rule: \\"The indicator introduces the necessary idea. That complete idea goes right of the arrow.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \\"g3\\",\\r\\n    n: 3,\\r\\n    title: \\"Negate sufficient\\",\\r\\n    words: [\\"unless\\", \\"until\\", \\"without\\"],\\r\\n    steps: [\\r\\n      \\"Choose one complete idea.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Negate it.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Put the negated idea on the left of the arrow.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Keep the other complete idea unchanged on the right.\\",\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    note: \\"Choosing the other starting idea gives you the contrapositive.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \\"g4\\",\\r\\n    n: 4,\\r\\n    title: \\"Negate necessary\\",\\r\\n    words: [\\"no\\", \\"none\\", \\"not both\\", \\"never\\", \\"cannot\\"],\\r\\n    steps: [\\r\\n      \\"Keep one complete idea unchanged on the left.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Negate the other complete idea.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Put that negation on the right.\\",\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    note: \\"Switching which complete idea you start with gives you the contrapositive.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n"}'}]

'co-lit' : ''}>Full credit<span>Sufficient: enough</span></div><b>→</b><div className=\"co-lit\">Passes<span>Necessary: required</span></div></div><p role=\"status\">{fact === 'credit' ? 'We know Maya passes. Earning full credit is enough under this rule.' : 'We know Maya passed. We still do not know her exact credit. Passing may happen another way.'}</p></div>;\n+}\n+type Translation = { word: string; sentence: string; target: string; other: string; left: string; right: string; why: string };\n+const examples: Record<string, Translation[]> = {\n+  'group-1': [\n+    { word: 'if', sentence: 'A report is reviewed if it is submitted before noon.', target: 'Submitted before noon', other: 'Reviewed', left: 'Submitted before noon', right: 'Reviewed', why: 'If what? Submission before noon. The sufficient clause comes second, but it still goes left.' },\n+    { word: 'when', sentence: 'When the alarm sounds, the staff evacuate.', target: 'Alarm sounds', other: 'Staff evacuate', left: 'Alarm sounds', right: 'Staff evacuate', why: 'When what? The alarm sounds. Under this stated rule, that event is enough for evacuation.' },\n+    { word: 'where', sentence: 'Where a station is available, the service operates.', target: 'Station available', other: 'Service operates', left: 'Station available', right: 'Service operates', why: 'Where what? A station is available. This rule makes station availability enough for service.' },\n+    { word: 'all', sentence: 'All registered guests receive a wristband.', target: 'Registered guest', other: 'Receives wristband', left: 'Registered guest', right: 'Receives wristband', why: 'All what? Registered guests. Keep registered; guest alone does not meet the whole condition.' },\n+    { word: 'the only', sentence: 'The only vehicles permitted inside are electric vehicles.', target: 'Vehicle permitted inside', other: 'Electric vehicle', left: 'Vehicle permitted inside', right: 'Electric vehicle', why: 'The only what? Electric alone is not enough for admission.' },\n+    { word: 'every', sentence: 'Every finalist receives an invitation.', target: 'Finalist', other: 'Receives invitation', left: 'Finalist', right: 'Receives invitation', why: 'Every what? Finalist. The rule covers each finalist without an exception.' },\n+    { word: 'any', sentence: 'Any applicant who submits late pays a fee.', target: 'Applicant submits late', other: 'Pays fee', left: 'Applicant submits late', right: 'Pays fee', why: 'Any what? A complaint is filed. Receives an invitation. Here always makes receiving one an exceptionless result of being a finalist.' },\n+    { word: 'requires', sentence: 'Entry requires a current pass.', target: 'Has current pass', other: 'Enters', left: 'Enters', right: 'Has current pass', why: 'Requires what?
