Keep current; an expired pass does not meet the condition.' },\n+    { word: 'must', sentence: 'A finalist must attend the interview.', target: 'Attends interview', other: 'Finalist', left: 'Finalist', right: 'Attends interview', why: 'Must what? Attend the interview. This sentence states a requirement. It does not establish that every finalist complied.' },\n+  ],\n+};\n+function TranslationLab({ id }: { id: string }) {\n+  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\n+  const [choice, setChoice] = useState<string | null>(null);\n+  const [diagram, setDiagram] = useState(false);\n+  const item = examples[id][index];\n+  return <div className=\"co-lab\"><p className=\"co-kicker\">Read the whole idea</p><div className=\"co-word-tabs\" aria-label=\"Indicator examples\">{examples[id].map((e, i) => <button key={e.word} aria-pressed={i === index} onClick={() => { setIndex(i); setChoice(null); setDiagram(false); }}>{e.word}</button>)}</div><blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote><p>What is <strong>{item.word}</strong> referring to?</p><div className=\"co-choices\">{[item.target, item.other].map(value => <button key={value} aria-pressed={choice === value} onClick={() => { setChoice(value); setDiagram(false); }}>{value}</button>)}</div>{choice && <div role=\"status\" className=\"co-feedback\"><strong>{choice === item.target ? 'You have the complete target.' : 'Read what the indicator refers to again.'}</strong><p>{item.why}</p>{choice === item.target && <button className=\"co-primary\" onClick={() => setDiagram(true)}>Place the target {id === 'group-1' ? 'left' : 'right'}</button>}</div>}{diagram && <Arrow left={item.left} right={item.right}/>}</div>;\n+}\n+function Flashcards({ id }: { id: string }) {\n+  const cards = examples[id];\n+  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\n+  const [back, setBack] = useState(false);\n+  const card = cards[index];\n+  return <div className=\"co-flash\"><p className=\"co-kicker\">Recall before revealing · {index + 1} / {cards.length}</p><button className={`co-flash-face ${back ? 'co-flipped' : ''}`} aria-label={back ? 'Hide answer' : 'Reveal answer'} onClick={() => setBack(!back)}>{back ? <><strong>{card.left} → {card.right}</strong><span>{card.why}</span></> : <><strong>{card.sentence}</strong><span>Say the complete rule. Then turn the card.</span></>}</button><div className=\"co-choices\"><button disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => { setIndex(index - 1); setBack(false); }}>Previous card</button><button onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % cards.length); setBack(false); }}>{index === cards.length - 1 ? 'Start again' : 'Next card'}</button></div><p className=\"co-small\">These cards help you recall the relationship. Your attached drill gives you separate questions.</p></div>;\n+}\n+function DirectionSwitch() {\n+  const [only, setOnly] = useState(false);\n+  return <div className=\"co-switch\"><div className=\"co-choices\"><button aria-pressed={!only} onClick={() => setOnly(false)}>if</button><button aria-pressed={only} onClick={() => setOnly(true)}>only if</button></div><blockquote>A student enters <mark>{only ? 'only if' : 'if'}</mark> the student has a pass.</blockquote><Arrow left={only ? 'Enters' : 'Has pass'} right={only ? 'Has pass' : 'Enters'}/><p role=\"status\">{only ? 'The pass is required. Having one does not establish entry.' : 'The pass is enough under this rule. Having one guarantees entry.'}</p></div>;\n+}\n+function Fundamentals({ roles = false }: { roles?: boolean }) {\n+  return <>\n+    {!roles && <><Section title=\"What is a conditional?\"><p>Take this statement: <strong>All dogs are mammals.</strong> Now suppose I tell you Luna is a dog. You know something else about her: she's a mammal.</p><p>You didn't need another fact about Luna. The rule covers every dog, and Luna belongs to that group.</p><blockquote>If something is a dog, then it is a mammal.</blockquote><p>That's a conditional. When one condition is met, another must be met too. It doesn't say usually. It leaves no room for a dog that isn't a mammal.</p><Arrow left=\"Dog\" right=\"Mammal\"/><p>Read the arrow as “if this is true, then that must be true.” The rule does not tell us that a particular dog exists. The separate fact about Luna lets us apply it to her.</p><SetExplorer/></Section><Section title=\"Why this matters on the LSAT\" dark><p>If I tell you Luna is a mammal, do you know she's a dog? She could be a cat. The rule allows that.</p><p>In a Must Be True question, you may apply a rule to a fact. In a Flaw question, someone may reverse it. In a Sufficient Assumption question, you may need a rule that connects evidence to a conclusion.</p><p>The question changes. You still need to know exactly what the rule gives you.</p><Check prompt=\"Every volunteer wears a badge. Ari wears a badge. Is Ari necessarily a volunteer?\" options={['Yes, the badge proves it.', 'No, Ari could have a badge for another reason.']} correct={1} explanation=\"Volunteer gives us badge. Badge does not give us volunteer. A staff member wearing a badge fits the rule too.\"/></Section></>}\n+    <Section title=\"Sufficient means enough\"><p>Being a dog is sufficient for being a mammal. It's enough. Once we know something is a dog, we have what we need to conclude it's a mammal.</p><p>Let's try another rule:</p><blockquote>If a student earns full credit, that student passes the assignment.</blockquote><p>Earning full credit is sufficient for passing. But it might not be the only way to pass. Enough doesn't mean the only way.</p><GateExplorer/><Check prompt=\"If a person wins the race, that person receives a medal. What is sufficient under this rule?\" options={['Winning the race', 'Receiving a medal']} correct={0} explanation=\"Winning is enough to guarantee a medal. The rule does not say that every medal recipient won.\"/></Section>\n+    <Section title=\"Necessary means required\"><p>Now look at the other side. A dog has to be a mammal. Being a mammal is necessary for being a dog.</p><p>Necessary means required. It doesn't mean enough.</p><blockquote>Entering the archive requires a pass.</blockquote><Arrow left=\"Enters the archive\" right=\"Has a pass\"/><p>The pass is required, so it goes right. Someone could have a pass and stay outside. That person doesn't break the rule.</p><div className=\"co-role-pair\"><div><h3>Sufficient</h3><p>Enough to guarantee the other side.</p><strong>Goes left</strong></div><div><h3>Necessary</h3><p>Required whenever the left side holds.</p><strong>Goes right</strong></div></div><p>These are roles within a particular rule. Something isn't permanently sufficient or necessary.</p><Check prompt=\"Every finalist receives an invitation. What is necessary for being a finalist under this rule?\" options={['Receiving an invitation', 'Being the winner']} correct={0} explanation=\"The rule requires an invitation for each finalist. An invitation alone does not establish finalist status.\"/></Section>\n+  </>;\n+}\n+function GroupLessons({ id }: { id: string }) {\n+  const group = GROUPS.find(g => g.id === (id === 'group-1' ? 'g1' : 'g2'))!;\n+  const first = id === 'group-1';\n+  return <><Section title={first ? 'Find the sufficient idea' : 'Find what is required'}><p>{first ? 'You understand the relationship. Now we can recognize the different ways a sentence expresses it. Group 1 points to the sufficient idea. That complete idea goes left.' : 'Group 2 points to the necessary idea. That complete idea goes right. We still read the sentence first; a word alone does not tell us the whole relationship.'}</p><div className=\"co-vocabulary\">{group.words.map(word => <span key={word}>{word}</span>)}</div><p>The question is: <strong>“What is the indicator referring to?”</strong> Add “what” wherever it makes grammatical sense: if what? Only if what? What is required?</p><blockquote>{first ? 'A student receives feedback if the student revises the essay.' : 'A pass is required for entry.'}</blockquote><p>{first ? 'If what? The student revises the essay. The sufficient idea appears second in the sentence. Moving a clause does not change the rule.' : 'What is required? A pass. The necessary idea appears before required. That is why we find its target instead of automatically taking the next words.'}</p><Arrow left={first ? 'Revises the essay' : 'Enters'} right={first ? 'Receives feedback' : 'Has a pass'}/></Section><Section title=\"Work through each expression\"><p>Start by finding the target. Then place it on its side of the arrow. Move through every expression below; notice how the wording changes while the relationship stays precise.</p><TranslationLab id={id}/></Section><Section title=\"Keep the whole condition\" dark><blockquote>{first ? 'Any applicant who submits late pays a fee.' : 'Entry requires a current pass.'}</blockquote><p>{first ? '“Applicant” alone is not the condition. The sentence is about an applicant who submits late. Keep that limit when you shorten the idea.' : 'A current pass is required. “Has a pass” drops current and permits an expired pass. Shortening should save writing, not change the rule.'}</p><Check prompt={first ? 'What goes left in this rule?' : 'What goes right in this rule?'} options={first ? ['Applicant', 'Applicant submits late'] : ['Has current pass', 'Has any pass']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} explanation={first ? 'The rule covers late-submitting applicants. We cannot apply it to every applicant.' : 'The requirement is a current pass. An expired one does not satisfy it.'}/></Section><Section title={first ? 'Read “the only” as a complete expression' : 'One word can change the direction'}>{first ? <><blockquote>The only people admitted are members.</blockquote><p>The only what? The only people admitted. Admitted is the sufficient idea.</p><Arrow left=\"Admitted\" right=\"Member\"/><p>Everyone admitted must be a member. It doesn't establish that every member gets in.</p><Check prompt=\"The only vehicles permitted inside are electric. Which diagram preserves the rule?\" options={['Electric → Permitted inside', 'Permitted inside → Electric']} correct={1} explanation=\"Permitted vehicles have to be electric. The sentence does not promise that every electric vehicle is permitted.\"/></> : <><DirectionSwitch/><p>Compare “only” with “the only” too. <strong>Only members are admitted.</strong> Only points to members, which go right. <strong>The only people admitted are members.</strong> The only points to admitted people, which go left. Both say <strong>Admitted → Member.</strong></p><Check prompt=\"Only registered guests receive wristbands. What follows from receiving a wristband?\" options={['The person is a registered guest.', 'Nothing about registration.']} correct={0} explanation=\"Only makes registration required for receiving a wristband. Compare the different rule: all registered guests receive wristbands.\"/></>}</Section><Section title=\"Say the rule without the scaffold\"><p>Before turning each card, identify both complete ideas and say which direction is justified. If you hesitate, use the What Test, then put yourself in the proposed sufficient condition.</p><Flashcards id={id}/></Section><Section title=\"Check the meaning, not just the word\"><Check prompt={first ? 'A report is reviewed if submitted before noon. Which is the same rule?' : 'A student graduates only if the project is completed. Which is the same rule?'} options={first ? ['If reviewed, it was submitted before noon.', 'If submitted before noon, it is reviewed.'] : ['If the student graduates, the project was completed.', 'If the project is completed, the student graduates.']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} explanation={first ? 'The if clause identifies the sufficient condition even when it comes last.' : 'Only if identifies a requirement. Completing the project is not stated enough to graduate.'}/><p>In the attached drill, work through a new set of sentences. Name the target, keep both ideas complete, and check that your arrow says what the sentence says.</p></Section></>;\n+}\n+export function ConditionalOpeningLesson({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {\n+  const group = unit.id === 'group-1' || unit.id === 'group-2';\n+  const title = group ? unit.id === 'group-1' ? 'Group 1: what is enough?' : 'Group 2: what is required?' : unit.id === 'orientation' ? 'What does the rule tell you?' : 'Enough. Required. Two different roles.';\n+  return <div className=\"conditional-note co-opening\"><DynamicIslandTOC key={unit.id} selector=\".co-opening [data-toc]\"/><header className=\"co-hero\"><p className=\"co-kicker\">Conditional logic</p><h1>{title}</h1><p>{group ? 'Read the sentence. Find the complete idea. Make its direction clear.' : 'Start with the relationship. The words and diagrams will follow.'}</p><a href=\"#co-reading\">Begin reading <span aria-hidden=\"true\">↓</span></a><div className=\"co-hero-orbit\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><span>Enough</span><i>→</i><span>Required</span></div></header><article id=\"co-reading\">{group ? <GroupLessons id={unit.id}/> : <Fundamentals roles={unit.id === 'sufficient-necessary'}/>}<footer className=\"co-footer\"><p>{reviewed ? 'You can return to these examples whenever you need.' : 'When you have worked through the examples, continue to your practice.'}</p><div className=\"co-choices\">{previousHref && <Link to={previousHref}>Previous lesson</Link>}<button onClick={onComplete}>{reviewed ? Which is equivalent?\', [\'Ticket issued → identification supplied.\', \'Identification supplied → ticket issued.\', \'Ticket not issued → no identification.\'], 0, \'First write no identification → not issued.\', [\'Negating the not-issued result gives issued.\', \'Its contrapositive requires identification.\']),\\r\\n302:        question(\'group-3-m2\', \'Unless the upload fails, the receipt appears. No receipt appears. What follows?\', [\'The upload fails.\', \'The upload succeeds.\', \'The upload was never attempted.\'], 0, \'Use the contrapositive of no failure → receipt appears.\', [\'Receipt absence excludes no upload failure.\', \'It therefore establishes failure under the stated rule, not a different explanation.\']),\\r\\n313:    id: \'group-4\', title: \'Group 4: Keep Two Conditions Apart\', area: \'translation\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'group-3\'],\\r\\n321:    method: [\'Identify the exclusion indicator and Group 4, checking exactly what coexistence is forbidden.\', \'Identify the two complete conditions that cannot coexist, preserving subjects, qualifiers and existing negatives.\', \'Assign symbols to those complete conditions.\', \'Apply the Group 4 rule: keep one unchanged on the left and negate the other on the right; cancel any resulting double negative.\', \'Find the equivalent contrapositive by reversing and negating both complete conditions.\', \'Translate both forms back to English and check that the rule excludes coexistence without inventing existence or an exactly-one requirement.\'],\\r\\n337:        question(\'group-4-a1\', \'No equipment not certified for outdoor use is deployed outdoors. Which follows?\', [\'Deployed outdoors → certified for outdoor use.\', \'Certified for outdoor use → deployed outdoors.\', \'Not deployed outdoors → not certified.\'], 0, \'Write not certified → not deployed, then contrapose.\', [\'Outdoor deployment excludes absence of outdoor certification.\', \'The resulting positive certification retains its original scope.\']),\\r\\n344:    id: \'mixed-translation\', title: \'Choose the Direction from the Meaning\', area: \'translation\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'group-4\'],\\r\\n351:    method: [\'Identify the indicator and group, using the What Test when its target is unclear.\', \'Identify both complete ideas, preserving subjects, scope and existing negatives.\', \'Assign symbols to the complete ideas.\', \'Apply the matching group translation rule; use a Personalized If/Then to check the meaning.\', \'Find the contrapositive, reversing and negating each complete condition.\', \'Recall and Rephrase: put the guide aside, write both forms in English, then compare with the original and explain any correction.\'],\\r\\n353:    guidedExample: question(\'mixed-translation-guide\', \'The server does not restart unless its update is complete. Which is equivalent?\', [\'Update complete → server restarts.\', \'Server restarts → update complete.\', \'Server does not restart → update incomplete.\'], 1, \'Write update incomplete → no restart first.\', [\'Incomplete update prevents restart.\', \'Its contrapositive makes completed update necessary for restart, not sufficient.\']),\\r\\n362:        question(\'mixed-translation-m1\', \'The exhibit remains open unless the alarm is activated. Which is equivalent?\', [\'Alarm activated → exhibit closes.\', \'Exhibit not open → alarm activated.\', \'Exhibit open → alarm not activated.\'], 1, \'Translate no activation → open, then contrapose.\', [\'No activation guarantees openness.\', \'Lack of openness therefore requires activation, but activation alone does not prove closure.\']),\\r\\n363:        question(\'mixed-translation-m2\', \'The only bids selected are bids with a deposit. Which inference is unsupported?\', [\'Selected → has deposit.\', \'No deposit → not selected.\', \'Has deposit → selected.\'], 2, \'A deposit is required, not promised sufficient.\', [\'The restriction gives selected → deposit and its contrapositive.\', \'The reverse would make every deposit-bearing bid selected without support.\']),\\r\\n367:        question(\'mixed-translation-a1\', \'No proposal not approved for the pilot is implemented in the pilot. Which translation is equivalent?\', [\'Implemented in pilot → approved for pilot.\', \'Approved for pilot → implemented in pilot.\', \'Implemented anywhere → approved for pilot.\'], 0, \'Cancel double negatives without broadening implementation scope.\', [\'Not approved for pilot → not implemented in pilot.\', \'Its contrapositive is pilot implementation → pilot approval.\']),\\r\\n374:    id: \'implicit-conditionals\', title: \'Recognize a Guarantee without a Familiar Indicator\', area: \'translation\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'mixed-translation\'],\\r\\n404:    id: \'direct-linking\', title: \'Connect Rules through the Same Condition\', area: \'linking\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'implicit-conditionals\'],\\r\\n410:      \'A link can be hidden until you take a contrapositive. Every tagged specimen is retained. Every discarded specimen is not retained. The second rule is equivalent to retained → not discarded. Now tagged → retained → not discarded is a forward chain. Write the equivalent rule first; do not simply reverse an arrow. If both sides already contain negatives, cancel the double negatives when contraposing.\',\\r\\n415:    method: [\'Translate each rule into complete conditions.\', \'Find a result-to-trigger match; if needed, form an equivalent contrapositive to expose it.\', \'Follow only forward arrows in the rules or their valid contrapositives to create a new rule.\', \'Apply a separately supplied case fact, or preserve the result as a rule if no fact is given.\'],\\r\\n433:        question(\'direct-linking-a3\', \'Every field-certified sensor is logged. Every uncalibrated sensor is unlogged. Every calibrated sensor has an identifier. Sensor F is field-certified. What follows?\', [\'F is calibrated and has an identifier.\', \'F is uncalibrated and has no identifier.\', \'Every identified sensor is field-certified.\'], 0, \'Expose a logged → calibrated link by contraposing the uncalibrated rule.\', [\'Uncalibrated → not logged becomes logged → calibrated after reversal and double-negation cancellation.\', \'F follows field-certified → logged → calibrated → has an identifier. No reverse identifier rule is supplied.\']),\\r\\n438:    id: \'no-link\', title: \'Know When Rules Do Not Connect\', area: \'linking\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'direct-linking\'],\\r\\n443:      \'An apparent mismatch may be real or may be resolved by a valid contrapositive. Check exact scope and subjects before declaring no link. The preceding linking lesson showed how contraposition exposes hidden connections. Use that procedure before deciding that a shared word supplies no valid path.\',\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c81666","wall_time_seconds":5.0112067,"session_id":89233,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4786bb","wall_time_seconds":0.4374803,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4989,"output":"  {\\r\\n    id: \'group-1\', title: \'Group 1: Identify What Guarantees What\', area: \'translation\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'contraposition\'],\\r\\n    goal: \'Translate sufficient-condition wording while preserving full predicates and contextual meaning.\',\\r\\n    teaching: [\\r\\n      \'Group 1 includes if, when, where, all, the only, every and any in their conditional uses. If a probe restarts, its log resets gives probe restarts → its log resets. The sufficient idea can appear second: its log resets whenever a probe restarts has the same direction.\',\\r\\n      \'Preserve the whole subject. Every permit renewed through the regional office receives a new number starts with permit renewed through the regional office, not permit. The modifier limits which permits receive the guarantee.\',\\r\\n      \'An indicator list is a starting aid. We asked if the gate was open is an indirect question, not an if-then rule. When I arrived, the gate was open reports an event. The only paintings exhibited are restored paintings means exhibited painting → restored painting. Read the whole grammatical claim rather than attaching an arrow to an isolated noun.\',\\r\\n      \'Use the What Test to identify an indicator target: ask if what, when what, or every what. In A report appears when a rover crosses the ridge, when what points to the rover crossing the ridge. Identify the complete idea, not just the nearest noun. Then check whether the sentence actually promises a result.\',\\r\\n      \'Recall and Rephrase practice: put the indicator list aside, recall the Group 1 role, and write the sentence as an if/then. Add its contrapositive in ordinary English. Compare with the original wording and explain any correction before trying another sentence.\',\\r\\n      \'Recognize related trigger phrasing: whenever, wherever, anytime, in the event that, and the moment that can introduce the triggering event. Each, everyone who, and everything that can specify a whole group. In the event that a sensor restarts, a new log begins means sensor restarts → new log begins. Preserve the complete event and group restrictions.\',\\r\\n      \'Provided that needs the whole promise: The archive will open provided that power is restored states restored power → archive opens. Guarantees and ensures can also connect a sufficient idea to its result. Leads to, produces, and results in express that direction only when the sentence makes an exceptionless claim; a usual tendency or mere causal possibility is weaker.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    method: [\'Identify the conditional indicator and Group 1; use the What Test to find its target.\', \'Identify the two complete ideas, including subjects, modifiers and existing negatives.\', \'Assign symbols to those complete ideas.\', \'Apply the Group 1 translation rule and check the guarantee against the sentence.\', \'Find the contrapositive by reversing and negating both complete conditions.\', \'Translate both forms back to English and compare their meaning with the original.\'],\\r\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'Any parcel collected from the north depot receives a receipt.\'], reasoning: [\'The trigger is being a parcel collected from that depot.\', \'The result is receiving a receipt for that same parcel.\'], conclusion: \'Parcel collected from north depot → receives receipt.\' },\\r\\n    guidedExample: question(\'group-1-guide\', \'A checkpoint report appears when a rover crosses the ridge. Before choosing, run the six steps: identify the indicator and group, name both complete ideas, assign symbols, translate, contrapose, and rephrase both forms in English. Which translates the rule?\', [\'Report appears → rover crosses ridge.\', \'Rover crosses ridge → checkpoint report appears.\', \'Rover does not cross ridge → no report appears.\'], 1, \'Ask when what: when a rover crosses the ridge. That is the sufficient event even though it appears second.\', [\'Crossing the ridge triggers the report.\', \'The sentence does not establish that reports occur only for crossings.\']),\\r\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Dropping a modifier or reversing the rule because the result appears first.\', repair: \'Write the complete if clause first. Let meaning, rather than word order, determine its role.\' },\\r\\n    drills: {\\r\\n      beginner: [\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-b1\', \'If a locker is reserved, it displays a nameplate. Choose the rule.\', [\'Reserved locker → displays nameplate.\', \'Nameplate displayed → reserved locker.\', \'Unreserved locker → no nameplate.\'], 0, \'Start with the complete idea after if.\', [\'Reservation is sufficient for the nameplate.\', \'The other arrows add a reverse or inverse.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-b2\', \'Every woven basket has a reinforced rim. What follows for a woven basket?\', [\'It has a reinforced rim.\', \'Every rimmed basket is woven.\', \'It is the only basket with a rim.\'], 0, \'Apply every in its stated direction.\', [\'The basket belongs to the woven group.\', \'That guarantees its reinforced rim without excluding other rimmed baskets.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-b3\', \'A notice is printed whenever the schedule changes. What triggers printing under this rule?\', [\'A notice being printed.\', \'The schedule changing.\', \'The schedule staying unchanged.\'], 1, \'Whenever introduces the sufficient idea.\', [\'The schedule change is the trigger.\', \'Printing is the promised result.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      medium: [\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-m1\', \'Any invoice submitted through the portal is time-stamped. Which translation preserves scope?\', [\'Invoice → time-stamped.\', \'Invoice submitted through portal → time-stamped.\', \'Time-stamped → submitted through portal.\'], 1, \'Keep how the invoice was submitted.\', [\'The rule covers portal-submitted invoices.\', \'It neither covers every invoice nor identifies the source of every time stamp.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-m2\', \'The only rehearsals recorded are rehearsals held in Studio B. Which follows?\', [\'Held in Studio B → recorded.\', \'Recorded rehearsal → held in Studio B.\', \'Held outside Studio B → recorded.\'], 1, \'Ask which rehearsals are excluded from the recorded group.\', [\'Recorded rehearsals are restricted to Studio B rehearsals.\', \'Studio B alone is not stated enough for recording.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-m3\', \'Where a monitoring station detects ice, its warning flag rises. A station\\\\\'s flag rises. What follows?\', [\'It detected ice.\', \'It detected no ice.\', \'Ice detection is unknown.\'], 2, \'A raised flag may have another cause.\', [\'Detection guarantees a flag, not the reverse.\', \'The raised flag therefore leaves detection unsettled.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      advanced: [\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-a1\', \'Every film selected for the outdoor series is captioned. Film V is selected for an indoor series. Which follows?\', [\'V is captioned.\', \'V is not captioned.\', \'Captioning is not settled by this rule.\'], 2, \'The series modifier belongs inside the trigger.\', [\'Indoor selection does not satisfy outdoor-series selection.\', \'The rule provides no captioning result for V.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-a2\', \'The curator asked if the painting was insured. Which rule can be extracted from that sentence alone?\', [\'Painting insured → curator asks.\', \'Curator asks → painting insured.\', \'Neither; it reports an inquiry.\'], 2, \'Ask whether if expresses a guarantee or an indirect question.\', [\'The curator asks about insurance status.\', \'The sentence asserts no conditional relationship between asking and insurance.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-1-a3\', \'If a component is not certified for heat exposure, it is stored indoors. Component E is not stored indoors. What follows?\', [\'E is certified for heat exposure.\', \'E is certified for every use.\', \'E is not certified for heat exposure.\'], 0, \'Use the contrapositive and cancel the negative.\', [\'No indoor storage excludes not certified for heat exposure.\', \'The result is certification for that specific exposure, not universal certification.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'group-2\', title: \'Group 2: Keep the Requirement on the Right\', area: \'translation\', phase: \'conditional\', prerequisites: [\'group-1\'],\\r\\n    goal: \'Translate necessary-condition wording without mistaking a requirement for a guarantee of success.\',\\r\\n    teaching: [\\r\\n      \'Group 2 includes only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must in their relevant uses. The vault opens only if its code is verified gives vault opens → code verified. Verification is required for opening, but may not be enough to open it.\',\\r\\n      \'Only members receive this newsletter restricts recipients to members: receives newsletter → member. Compare the only recipients are members, a Group 1 grammatical form with the same result. Meaning decides the direction.\',\\r\\n      \'Read requires and must carefully. Certification requires an exam pass states a requirement for certification. A staff member must wear a badge states an obligation; a person can violate it. Do not turn must wear into actually wears unless compliance is separately assumed. Always can describe a universal pattern; its placement and scope still matter.\',\\r\\n      \'The What Test matters when the target is not immediately after the indicator. A charged battery is required for activation: what is required? A charged battery. Activation requires a charged battery: requires what? A charged battery. Both express activation → charged battery. Word position changes; the requirement does not.\',\\r\\n      \'Use a Personalized If/Then to check your translation. Imagine the display has activated. This rule forces you to know that its battery is charged. Imagine only a charged battery instead: activation is not forced. Keep the original scope and do not fill in an unstated guarantee.\',\\r\\n      \'Recognize required, prerequisite, necessary, essential, needs, and depends on as requirement phrasing when that is their meaning. A seal is essential for pressure retention gives pressure retained → seal present. Pressure retention depends on a seal gives the same direction. Ask what is required even when the requirement appears first.\',\\r\\n      \'An expression can name the result without belonging to one fixed word group. Activation implies a recorded event means activation → recorded event, as does Activation means a recorded event under an explicit catalogue convention. Then and will can introduce the result of an if clause. Signals may describe evidence rather than certainty. Identify the guarantee in the full sentence instead of assigning direction from one word alone.\',\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    method: [\'Identify the conditional indicator and Group 2; ask what it requires or restricts using the What Test.\', \'Identify the two complete ideas, preserving permission, obligation, qualifiers and negatives.\', \'Assign symbols to those complete ideas.\', \'Translate the restricted event on the left and its requirement on the right; verify with a Personalized If/Then.\', \'Find the contrapositive by reversing and negating both complete conditions.\', \'Translate both forms back to English without turning a requirement into a guarantee of success.\'],\\r\\n    workedExample: { statements: [\'A package is released only after its clearance is recorded.\'], reasoning: [\'Release requires recorded clearance.\', \'Recorded clearance alone does not promise release.\'], conclusion: \'Package released → its clearance recorded.\' },\\r\\n    guidedExample: question(\'group-2-guide\', \'Only subscribers can access the recording. Before choosing, identify what only restricts, run the six steps, and check your direction with a Personalized If/Then about having access permission. Which follows?\', [\'Subscriber → can access recording.\', \'Can access recording → subscriber.\', \'Subscriber → has accessed recording.\'], 1, \'If I can access the recording, what must be true of me? I must be a subscriber. Being a subscriber alone does not guarantee access.\', [\'Access permission is restricted to subscribers.\', \'The claim guarantees neither permission for every subscriber nor an actual access event.\']),\\r\\n    commonMistake: { mistake: \'Using a requirement as sufficient for the restricted result.\', repair: \'Read only if as cannot occur without. Then ask what additional requirements could still exist.\' },\\r\\n    drills: {\\r\\n      beginner: [\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-b1\', \'The display activates only if the battery is charged. Translate.\', [\'Display activates → battery charged.\', \'Battery charged → display activates.\', \'Display inactive → battery uncharged.\'], 0, \'Only if introduces the requirement.\', [\'Activation requires charging.\', \'Charging is not stated enough for activation.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-b2\', \'Only residents receive a parking sticker. What is necessary for receiving one?\', [\'Being a resident.\', \'Driving daily.\', \'Already having a sticker.\'], 0, \'Identify the group to which recipients are restricted.\', [\'Recipients must be residents under this rule.\', \'No guarantee is made for every resident.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-b3\', \'Every registered buoy always broadcasts its identifier. Which rule follows?\', [\'Broadcasts identifier → registered buoy.\', \'Registered buoy → broadcasts identifier.\', \'Unregistered buoy → no broadcast.\'], 1, \'Always describes what registered buoys do.\', [\'Registration is the starting condition.\', \'Broadcasting is the universal result assigned to that group.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      medium: [\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-m1\', \'Entry to the final round requires a qualifying score. Mira has a qualifying score. What follows?\', [\'Mira enters the final round.\', \'Mira cannot enter.\', \'Entry is not established.\'], 2, \'A required score might not be the only requirement.\', [\'A qualifying score is necessary for entry.\', \'The rule does not make it sufficient.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-m2\', \'The camera records only when motion is detected. No motion is detected. What follows?\', [\'The camera does not record.\', \'The camera records.\', \'The camera is broken.\'], 0, \'Use absence of the required detection.\', [\'Recording would require motion detection.\', \'Its absence excludes recording without identifying a fault.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-m3\', \'The beacon operates only where satellite contact is available. It operates at Site P. What follows?\', [\'Satellite contact is available at P.\', \'It operates at all sites with contact.\', \'P is the only site with contact.\'], 0, \'Keep where as a restriction on the operation site.\', [\'Operation at P requires contact there.\', \'No claim identifies every operation site or every contact site.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      advanced: [\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-a1\', \'Only reviewers appointed for the emergency panel may inspect these records. Nia is a reviewer appointed to another panel. Which follows?\', [\'Nia may inspect these records.\', \'This rule excludes Nia\\\\\'s permission unless she also has the emergency appointment.\', \'Nia cannot be a reviewer.\'], 1, \'The appointment modifier is part of the necessary condition.\', [\'Inspection permission requires the emergency-panel appointment.\', \'Another appointment alone does not meet that requirement.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-a2\', \'Agency policy obliges every holder of a night-filming permit to submit a signed safety declaration. Rowan holds a night-filming permit. What follows?\', [\'Rowan is obliged to submit a signed safety declaration.\', \'Rowan actually submitted a signed declaration.\', \'Everyone who submits a signed declaration receives a night-filming permit.\'], 0, \'An explicit policy obligation does not establish compliance.\', [\'Rowan is a holder covered by the stated policy.\', \'The policy establishes Rowan\\\\\'s obligation, not whether Rowan performed it.\']),\\r\\n        question(\'group-2-a3\', \'A claim is eligible only if it was not filed after the deadline. Claim Q was filed after the deadline. What follows?\', [\'Q is eligible.\', \'Q is not eligible.\', \'Every timely claim is eligible.\'], 1, \'Negate the negative necessary condition.\', [\'Eligibility requires not filed after deadline.\', \'Filing after deadline defeats that requirement and excludes eligibility.\']),\\r\\n      ],\\r\\nimport { writeFileSync, mkdirSync, readFileSync } from \'node:fs\';\\r\\nimport { resolve } from \'node:path\';\\r\\nimport { foundationUnits } from \'../../src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/foundations.source\';\\r\\nimport { applicationUnits } from \'../../src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/applications.source\';\\r\\nimport { validateConditionalContent } from \'../../src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/validate\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst units = [...foundationUnits, ...applicationUnits];\\r\\nconst errors = validateConditionalContent(units);\\r\\nif (errors.length) throw new Error(`Conditional content validation failed:\\\\n${errors.join(\'\\\\n\')}`);\\r\\nconst directory = resolve(\'src/bootcamps/conditionals/data\');\\r\\nconst output = JSON.stringify(units, null, 2) + \'\\\\n\';\\r\\nconst destination = resolve(directory, \'curriculum.generated.json\');\\r\\nif (process.argv.includes(\'--check\')) {\\r\\n  if (readFileSync(destination, \'utf8\').replace(/\\\\r\\\\n/g, \'\\\\n\') !== output) {\\r\\n    throw new Error(\'Conditional curriculum bundle is stale. Run npm run content:conditionals.\');\\r\\n  }\\r\\n} else {\\r\\n  mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true });\\r\\n  writeFileSync(destination, output);\\r\\n}\\r\\nprocess.stdout.write(`Generated ${units.length} lessons and ${units.reduce((count, unit) => count + Object.values(unit.drills).flat().length, 0)} practice questions.\\\\n`);\\r\\nimport { DIFFICULTIES, type ConditionalUnit } from \'./types\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Content checks enforce completeness and identity, not proof of logical correctness. */\\r\\nexport function validateConditionalContent(units: ConditionalUnit[]): string[] {\\r\\n  const errors: string[] = [];\\r\\n  const unitIds = new Set(units.map(unit => unit.id));\\r\\n  const questionIds = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  const practicePrompts = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  if (units.length !== 30 || unitIds.size !== 30) errors.push(\'Expected 30 distinct learning units.\');\\r\\n  units.forEach(unit => {\\r\\n    if (!unit.goal || unit.teaching.length < 2 || unit.method.length < 2 ||\\r\\n        !unit.workedExample.statements.length || !unit.workedExample.reasoning.length || !unit.workedExample.conclusion ||\\r\\n        !unit.commonMistake.mistake || !unit.commonMistake.repair) errors.push(`${unit.id}: incomplete teaching.`);\\r\\n    unit.prerequisites.forEach(id => { if (!unitIds.has(id) || id === unit.id) errors.push(`${unit.id}: invalid prerequisite ${id}.`); });\\r\\n    const questions = [unit.guidedExample, ...DIFFICULTIES.flatMap(level => unit.drills[level])];\\r\\n    DIFFICULTIES.forEach(level => { if (unit.drills[level]?.length < 3) errors.push(`${unit.id}/${level}: fewer than three fresh practice items.`); });\\r\\n    questions.forEach(question => {\\r\\n      if (!question.id || questionIds.has(question.id)) errors.push(`${unit.id}: repeated or missing question ID ${question.id}.`);\\r\\n      questionIds.add(question.id);\\r\\n      if (question.options.length < 2 || new Set(question.options).size !== question.options.length ||\\r\\n          !Number.isInteger(question.answerIndex) || question.answerIndex < 0 || question.answerIndex >= question.options.length)\\r\\n        errors.push(`${question.id}: invalid answer options.`);\\r\\n      if (!question.prompt || !question.explanation || !question.hint || question.reasoningSteps.length < 2)\\r\\n        errors.push(`${question.id}: missing teaching feedback.`);\\r\\n      if (question !== unit.guidedExample) {\\r\\n        const normalized = question.prompt.replace(/\\\\s+/g, \' \').trim().toLowerCase();\\r\\n        if (practicePrompts.has(normalized)) errors.push(`${question.id}: repeated practice prompt.`);\\r\\n        practicePrompts.add(normalized);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    if (JSON.stringify(unit).includes(\'\\\\u2014\')) errors.push(`${unit.id}: prohibited em dash.`);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  const firstQuantity = units.findIndex(unit => unit.phase === \'quantifier\');\\r\\n  if (firstQuantity !== 26 || units.slice(firstQuantity).some(unit => unit.phase !== \'quantifier\')) errors.push(\'Quantifier phase must follow all 26 conditional units.\');\\r\\n  return errors;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: exec

DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET, resolveConditionalAssignment, type ConditionalAssignmentTarget } from \'@/lib/conditionalBootcampAssignment\';\\r\\nimport { classroomItemMatchesBootcamp } from \'@/lib/bootcamps\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface ConditionalAssignmentRow {\\r\\n  id: string; student_id: string; type: string; title: string;\\r\\n  source_id: string | null; metadata_json: string; route: string; target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget;\\r\\n  unitIds: string[]; mode: \'learn\' | \'practice\'; difficulty?: ConditionalDifficulty;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction parseTarget(value: unknown): ConditionalAssignmentTarget | null {\\r\\n  if (!value || typeof value !== \'object\' || Array.isArray(value)) return null;\\r\\n  const target = value as ConditionalAssignmentTarget;\\r\\n  if (![\'course\', \'stage\', \'area\', \'skill\', \'general\'].includes(target.scope) ||\\r\\n    ![\'learn\', \'practice\'].includes(target.mode) || !DIFFICULTIES.includes(target.difficulty)) return null;\\r\\n  if ((target.scope === \'skill\' || target.scope === \'stage\') && !findConditionalUnit(target.unitId)) return null;\\r\\n  if (target.scope === \'area\' && !CONDITIONAL_AREAS.some(area => area.id === target.area)) return null;\\r\\n  return target;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction legacyTarget(source: string | null): ConditionalAssignmentTarget | null {\\r\\n  if (!source || !source.startsWith(\'/bootcamp/conditionals\')) return null;\\r\\n  const url = new URL(source, \'https://local.invalid\');\\r\\n  const base = \'/bootcamp/conditionals\';\\r\\n  if (url.pathname !== base && !url.pathname.startsWith(`${base}/`)) return null;\\r\\n  const level = url.searchParams.get(\'level\') ?? \'beginner\';\\r\\n  if (!DIFFICULTIES.includes(level as ConditionalDifficulty)) return null;\\r\\n  const difficulty = level as ConditionalDifficulty;\\r\\n  const start = url.searchParams.get(\'start\');\\r\\n  if (start) return parseTarget({ scope: \'stage\', mode: \'learn\', difficulty, unitId: start });\\r\\n  const pathUnit = url.pathname.match(/^\\\\/bootcamp\\\\/conditionals\\\\/(?:lessons|activities)\\\\/([^/]+)$/)?.[1];\\r\\n  const unitId = pathUnit ?? url.searchParams.get(\'unit\');\\r\\n  const area = url.searchParams.get(\'area\');\\r\\n  const mode = url.pathname === `${base}/practice` ? \'practice\' : \'learn\';\\r\\n  if (unitId) return parseTarget({ scope: \'skill\', mode, difficulty, unitId });\\r\\n  if (area) return parseTarget({ scope: \'area\', mode, difficulty, area });\\r\\n  if (url.pathname === `${base}/practice`) return parseTarget({ scope: \'general\', mode: \'practice\', difficulty });\\r\\n  return url.pathname === base && !url.search ? { ...DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET } : null;\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport function conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget): string[] {\\r\\n  if (target.scope === \'course\') return getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits).map(unit => unit.id);\\r\\n  if (target.scope === \'stage\') return getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits, target.unitId).map(unit => unit.id);\\r\\n  return conditionalUnits.filter(unit => target.scope === \'skill\' ? unit.id === target.unitId\\r\\n    : target.scope === \'area\' ? unit.area === target.area : unit.phase === \'conditional\').map(unit => unit.id);\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport async function resolveConditionalAssignments(rows: ConditionalAssignmentRow[], userId: string, now = Date.now()): Promise<ResolvedConditionalAssignment[]> {\\r\\n  const result: ResolvedConditionalAssignment[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const row of rows) {\\r\\n    if (row.student_id !== userId || row.type !== \'bootcamp_module\' || !row.visible_to_student ||\\r\\n      ![\'assigned\', \'in_progress\', \'completed\'].includes(row.status) || !classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(row, \'conditionals\')) continue;\\r\\n    if (row.unlock_at && (!Number.isFinite(Date.parse(row.unlock_at)) || ## assistant

Implemented opening lessons through Group 2.

- Orientation: conditional meaning, rule versus fact, direction, LSAT purpose.
- Sufficient/necessary: separate teaching beats with interactive role comparison.
- Groups 1/2: all seven indicators each, worked examples, target identification, guided diagrams, independent diagram checks, flashcards, consolidation.
- Added animated subset visual, reduced-motion support, stable TOC anchors, responsive styling.
- Preserved obligation wording for “must.”
- Reordered course opening while preserving legacy stage assignment membership.
- Integrated your media component before the footer.
- Added DEV-only preview: `/dev/conditional-opening-preview?unit=orientation`.

Checks: typecheck and direct Vite build passed before final media integration; 40 relevant tests passed across runs. Canonical-content build check passed. Final build/typecheck should run after media integration.

No push/deploy. Original three dirty files preserved. Existing student-facing level labels remain in older practice/guided route UI for your final review.

Write the rule.\', answer: \'Complaint filed → Review occurs. “Only when” makes the review necessary for the complaint in this stated rule.\' },\\r\\n  ],\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Recall({ items }: { items: Card[] }) {\\r\\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [revealed, setRevealed] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const item = items[index];\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"co-flash\\">\\r\\n    <p className=\\"co-kicker\\">A fresh example · {index + 1} of {items.length}</p>\\r\\n    <button type=\\"button\\" className={`co-flash-face ${revealed ? \'co-flipped\' : \'\'}`} aria-label={revealed ? \'Hide recall explanation\' : \'Reveal recall explanation\'} aria-expanded={revealed} onClick={() => setRevealed(!revealed)}>\\r\\n      <strong>{revealed ? item.answer : item.question}</strong>\\r\\n      {!revealed && <span>Answer in your own words, then compare.</span>}\\r\\n    </button>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\">\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => { setIndex(index - 1); setRevealed(false); }}>Previous example</button>\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % items.length); setRevealed(false); }}>{index === items.length - 1 ? \'Start these again\' : \'Next example\'}</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ConditionalOpeningMedia({ unitId }: { unitId: string }) {\\r\\n  const items = cards[unitId];\\r\\n  if (!items) return null;\\r\\n  return <section id=\\"co-return-to-the-relationship\\" className=\\"co-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title=\\"Return to the relationship\\">\\r\\n    <h2>Return to the relationship</h2>\\r\\n    <p>Try these before moving on. Say what the rule gives you and explain why.</p>\\r\\n    <Recall key={unitId} items={items}/>\\r\\n    {unitId === \'sufficient-necessary\' && <details className=\\"co-media-summary\\">\\r\\n      <summary>See the relationships together</summary>\\r\\n      <figure style={{ margin: \'24px 0 0\' }}>\\r\\n        <svg viewBox=\\"0 0 700 400\\" className=\\"co-overview\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-labelledby=\\"co-order-overview-title co-order-overview-desc\\">\\r\\n          <title id=\\"co-order-overview-title\\">Marked ready is sufficient. Packed is necessary.</title>\\r\\n          <desc id=\\"co-order-overview-desc\\">Every order marked ready has been packed. Marked ready implies packed. Knowing packed alone leaves ready status unknown. The ready group is contained in the packed group, with no claim about unequal group sizes.</desc>\\r\\n          <rect width=\\"700\\" height=\\"400\\" rx=\\"22\\" fill=\\"#193b40\\"/>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"40\\" y=\\"52\\" fill=\\"#f6f3e7\\" fontSize=\\"25\\" fontFamily=\\"Georgia\\">Every order marked ready has been packed.</text>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"80\\" y=\\"112\\" fill=\\"#b9dbc9\\" fontSize=\\"25\\" fontFamily=\\"Georgia\\">Marked ready</text><text x=\\"325\\" y=\\"112\\" fill=\\"#f6f3e7\\" fontSize=\\"34\\">→</text><text x=\\"450\\" y=\\"112\\" fill=\\"#edcda0\\" fontSize=\\"25\\" fontFamily=\\"Georgia\\">Packed</text>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"80\\" y=\\"147\\" fill=\\"#f6f3e7\\" fontSize=\\"16\\">Sufficient: enough</text><text x=\\"450\\" y=\\"147\\" fill=\\"#f6f3e7\\" fontSize=\\"16\\">Necessary: required</text>\\r\\n          <rect x=\\"40\\" y=\\"178\\" width=\\"620\\" height=\\"151\\" rx=\\"18\\" fill=\\"#d7e9df\\"/>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"365\\" y=\\"210\\" fill=\\"#162d32\\" fontSize=\\"20\\">Packed orders</text>\\r\\n          <rect x=\\"70\\" y=\\"204\\" width=\\"265\\" height=\\"100\\" rx=\\"16\\" fill=\\"#fcf3df\\" stroke=\\"#bd8a47\\"/>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"90\\" y=\\"241\\" fill=\\"#162d32\\" fontSize=\\"20\\">Orders marked ready</text>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"90\\" y=\\"276\\" fill=\\"#162d32\\" fontSize=\\"17\\">Order A: packed follows</text>\\r\\n          <text x=\\"40\\" y=\\"367\\" fill=\\"#f6f3e7\\" fontSize=\\"17\\">Packed alone: the ready label remains unknown.</text>\\r\\n        </svg>\\r\\n        <figcaption className=\\"co-small\\">Read the arrow in its stated direction. You know something else about it: it has been packed.</p><p>You didn\'t need to open the box. The rule covers every order marked ready, and Order A has that label.</p><blockquote>If an order is marked ready, then it has been packed.</blockquote><p>That\'s a conditional. When one condition is met, another must be met too. It doesn\'t say usually. Under this rule, an order cannot be marked ready without being packed.</p><Arrow left=\\"Marked ready\\" right=\\"Packed\\"/><p>Read the arrow as “if this is true, then that must be true.” The rule does not tell us that any order is marked ready. It\'s enough. Once we know the order is marked ready, this rule gives us what we need to conclude it has been packed.</p><p>Keep the same rule:</p><blockquote>Every order marked ready has been packed.</blockquote><p>The ready label is enough to establish packing. An order marked ready has to be packed. Being packed is necessary for being marked ready under this rule.</p><p>Necessary means required. It doesn\'t mean enough.</p><blockquote>Entering the archive requires a pass.</blockquote><Arrow left=\\"Enters the archive\\" right=\\"Has a pass\\"/><p>The pass is required, so it goes right. Someone could have a pass and stay outside. That person doesn\'t break the rule.</p><div className=\\"co-role-pair\\"><div><h3>Sufficient</h3><p>Enough to guarantee the other side.</p><strong>Goes left</strong></div><div><h3>Necessary</h3><p>Required whenever the left side holds.</p><strong>Goes right</strong></div></div><p>These are roles within a particular rule. Now we can recognize the different ways a sentence expresses it. Group 1 points to the sufficient idea. That complete idea goes left.\' : \'Group 2 points to the necessary idea. That complete idea goes right. We still read the sentence first; a word alone does not tell us the whole relationship.\'}</p><div className=\\"co-vocabulary\\">{group.words.map(word => <span key={word}>{word}</span>)}</div><p>The question is: <strong>“What is the indicator referring to?”</strong> Add “what” wherever it makes grammatical sense: if what? Only if what? What is required?</p><blockquote>{first ? \'A student receives feedback if the student revises the essay.\' : \'A pass is required for entry.\'}</blockquote><p>{first ? \'If what? The student revises the essay. The sufficient idea appears second in the sentence. Moving a clause does not change the rule.\' : \'What is required? A pass. The necessary idea appears before required. That checks the direction before you commit to it.</p><DiagramBuilder id={id}/></Section><Section title=\\"Say the rule without the scaffold\\"><p>Before turning each card, identify both complete ideas and say which direction is justified. If you hesitate, use the What Test, then put yourself in the proposed sufficient condition.</p><Flashcards id={id}/></Section><Section title=\\"Check the meaning, not just the word\\"><Check prompt={first ? \'A report is reviewed if submitted before noon. Which is the same rule?\' : \'A student graduates only if the project is completed. Which is the same rule?\'} options={first ? [\'If reviewed, it was submitted before noon.\', \'If submitted before noon, it is reviewed.\'] : [\'If the student graduates, the project was completed.\', \'If the project is completed, the student graduates.\']} correct={first ? 1 : 0} explanation={first ? \'The if clause identifies the sufficient condition even when it comes last.\' : \'Only if identifies a requirement. Completing the project is not stated enough to graduate.\'}/><p>In the attached drill, work through a new set of sentences. Name the target, keep both ideas complete, and check that your arrow says what the sentence says.</p></Section></>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport function ConditionalOpeningLesson({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-choices\\">\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" disabled={index === 0} onClick={() => { setIndex(index - 1); setRevealed(false); }}>Previous example</button>\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % items.length); setRevealed(false); }}>{index === items.length - 1 ? \'Start these again\' : \'Next example\'}</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ConditionalOpeningMedia({ unitId }: { unitId: string }) {\\r\\n  const items = cards[unitId];\\r\\n  if (!items) return null;\\r\\n  return <section id=\\"co-return-to-the-relationship\\" className=\\"co-section\\" data-toc data-toc-title=\\"Return to the relationship\\">\\r\\n    <h2>Return to the relationship</h2>\\r\\n    <p>Try these before moving on. Say what the rule gives you and explain why.</p>\\r\\n    <Recall key={unitId} items={items}/>\\r\\n    {unitId === \'sufficient-necessary\' && <details className=\\"co-media-summary\\">\\r\\n      <summary>See the relationships together</summary>\\r\\n      <figure className=\\"co-relationship-overview\\">\\r\\n        <h3>Every order marked ready has been packed.</h3>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"co-overview-roles\\"><div><strong>Marked ready</strong><span>Sufficient: enough</span></div><b aria-hidden=\\"true\\">→</b><span className=\\"co-sr-only\\">implies</span><div><strong>Packed</strong><span>Necessary: required</span></div></div>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"co-overview-set\\">\\r\\n          <svg viewBox=\\"0 0 600 250\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-labelledby=\\"co-order-overview-title co-order-overview-desc\\">\\r\\n            <title id=\\"co-order-overview-title\\">Marked ready is sufficient. Packed is necessary.</title>\\r\\n            <desc id=\\"co-order-overview-desc\\">The ready group is contained within packed orders. All ready orders are packed. Packed alone does not establish the ready label. The drawing does not require unequal group sizes.</desc>\\r\\n            <rect x=\\"10\\" y=\\"10\\" width=\\"580\\" height=\\"230\\" rx=\\"24\\" fill=\\"#d7e9df\\"/>\\r\\n            <rect x=\\"40\\" y=\\"78\\" width=\\"285\\" height=\\"132\\" rx=\\"18\\" fill=\\"#fcf3df\\" stroke=\\"#bd8a47\\"/>\\r\\n          </svg>\\r\\n          <span className=\\"co-overview-packed-label\\">Packed orders</span><span className=\\"co-overview-ready-label\\">Orders marked ready</span>\\r\\n"}'}]

Having one guarantees entry.\'}</p></div>;\\r\\n122:  const items = diagramExamples[id];\\r\\n127:  return <div className=\\"co-lab\\"><p className=\\"co-kicker\\">Build the diagram</p><blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote><p>Both complete ideas are below. Choose the one that belongs on the left. The remaining idea goes right.</p><div className=\\"co-choices\\">{[item.target, item.other].map(value => <button key={value} aria-pressed={left === value} onClick={() => { setLeft(value); setChecked(false); }}>{value}</button>)}</div>{left && <><Arrow left={left} right={left === item.target ? item.other : item.target}/><button className=\\"co-primary\\" onClick={() => setChecked(true)}>Check my diagram</button></>}<div role=\\"status\\">{checked && <div className={`co-feedback ${left === item.left ? \'co-correct\' : \'\'}`}><strong>{left === item.left ? \'Your arrow preserves the relationship.\' : \'Your arrow reverses this relationship.\'}</strong><p>{item.why}</p><p>{item.readback}</p><button onClick={() => { setIndex((index + 1) % items.length); setLeft(null); setChecked(false); }}>{index === items.length - 1 ? \'Practice these again\' : \'Next sentence\'}</button></div>}</div></div>;\\r\\n"}'}]
